# Quirks to preserve

The port is an exact replica of Spelunky Classic 1.1: the same state as the original, tick for tick, **bugs
included**. This is the living list of the bugs in the 1.1 source and the oddities of the GameMaker 8 runner
(and its renderer) that the port has to reproduce, plus the places where the reference oracle itself isn't GM8.
When you port something that looks wrong but is what 1.1 does, add it here.

Each entry says what happens, where (a GML location, or the runner behaviour), how we know, and which test pins
it; entries the `gm` runtime already reproduces also say where (§5). GML paths are relative to `assets/gml/`
(regenerate with `scripts/extract`); OpenGMK paths are under `gm8emulator/src/` at the commit pinned in
[`tools/oracle/common.sh`](../tools/oracle/common.sh). Port paths are relative to the crate's `src/` (`crates/gm`
in §5, `crates/audio` in §8).

**Status** is one of:

- **GML**: read in the 1.1 source. True by definition, but the runner rules it relies on may be assumed.
- **OpenGMK**: confirmed in OpenGMK's source (file named). OpenGMK models GM8; it isn't GM8.
- **Oracle**: verified against an oracle run (trace or screenshot).
- **GM8**: run on the real GM8 runner (Spelunky.exe's) under Wine, with `tools/gm8-probe`. Under Rosetta, so
  transcendentals carry the caveat in [the probe README](../tools/gm8-probe/README.md#emulation).
- **DLL**: read in `supersound.dll`'s disassembly (PureBasic's Sound library over DirectSound).
- **Assumed**: neither yet. Verify before relying on it.

Most runner rules are "OpenGMK" until a trace confirms them; [runtime.md §14](design/runtime.md#14-verify-against-oracle)
lists the oracle checks still to do.

## Fixes go behind off-by-default options

Never change what 1.1 does by default, even when it's plainly a bug. A fix (or a deliberate deviation, such as
splitting the RNG stream for seeded levels) goes behind an option that is **off by default**, and with it off
the port must still match the oracle bit for bit. Name the option after the quirk, link its entry here, and
test both settings. Replays, traces and savestates must record which options were on, so a run with fixes enabled
is never compared against the oracle as if it were 1.1. No such options exist yet.

Where the oracle itself can't be GM8 (§7), the port follows a recorded decision instead; those aren't fixes.

## 1. Bugs and leftovers in the 1.1 source

| Quirk | Where | Status | Test |
|---|---|---|---|
| `rand(a, b)` writes `randNum` as an **instance variable** of the caller (or the `with` target), because it isn't declared `var`. 1,363 call sites, so nearly every instance gets a `randNum`; nothing reads it, but it shows up in `--fields` traces and in "variable exists" checks. | `scripts/rand.gml` | GML | test TODO |
| `instance_activate_region` is passed `x2, y2` where it takes `width, height`, so the reactivated region around the player is far larger than 32x32 and grows with the player's position. Objects to the right of and below the player stay active far outside the view: a bat in `action-throw-rock-snake.txt` is active for 63 frames from 870 while more than 96 px right of the view, always inside the oversized region. | `objects/oLevel/step.gml:94` | GML + Oracle | `action-throw-rock-snake.txt`, `enemy-mines-seed-1-kills.txt` (every frame's active set is compared) |
| The pause screen's keyboard keys aren't only the ones it shows (ESC-RETURN F1-DIE F10-QUIT): `oScreen`'s begin step reads `checkBombPressed` and `checkRopePressed`, which take the keyboard's bomb and rope keys as well as the gamepad's, so the bomb key (A) kills the player like F1 and the rope key (S) quits like F10. The two deaths differ: the bomb path scales the music back by `global.musicVol` and stops it only without the ankh; F1 sets it to 10000 and always stops it. | `objects/oScreen/step_begin.gml:5-30`, `keypress_F1.gml` | GML + Oracle | `flow-pause-game-over-new-game.txt` (F1 and A), `ending-seed-1.txt` (S); wiki clip `quirks/pause-bomb-key-dies` |
| **The Tutorial's pause screen shows a negative depth.** `oScreen`'s begin step writes the pause screen's depth as `174.8*(global.currLevel-1)+(py+8)*0.34` feet, with `py` the player's `y` when the game paused, on every level `isLevel()` takes, the Tutorial included (which it names TUTORIAL CAVE). The Tutorial is level 0 (both of the title's ways in, the START door on a first game and the TUTORIAL door, set `currLevel = 0`), so the depth starts 174.8 feet below zero, and it would take a `py` past 506 to reach zero, in a room 304 px tall. Where the player starts (`y` 72) it reads DEPTH: -147.60 FEET, and standing on the lower cave's floor, the lowest floor he can stand on (`y` 264), -82.32. Reached by pausing in the Tutorial. | `objects/oScreen/step_begin.gml:55,85-91`, `objects/oPlayer1/other_animation_end.gml:72-95` | GML + Oracle | `flow-tutorial-pause-depth.txt` (paused where he starts, `py` 72 on 568, and on the lower cave's floor, `py` 264 on 980, with `currLevel` 0); `flow-tutorial-title.txt` (`py` 72 on 800); wiki clip `quirks/tutorial-negative-depth` |
| `SS_Unload` is bound to the DLL's `SS_Init` export, so the game-end handlers that "unload" SuperSound call its init again. What that does to the DLL is in §8. | `scripts/SS_Init.gml:11-13`; called from `other_game_end` of `oMusic`, `oScreen`, `core` | GML | see §8 |
| Dangling references to `config.exe` resources: `room_goto(rInit)` and `oInit.startGame = true`. `rInit` and `oInit` exist only in `config.gmk`, so reaching this code raises GML's unknown-variable error. None of it is reachable in 1.1: `rKeyConfig` and `rJoyConfig` are entered only from `oKeyConfigButton` and `oJoyConfigButton`, which, like `oOKButton`, no room places and no code creates, and nothing calls `room_goto_next` next to them in the room order. Sent there by hand, each key-config press binds two keys (§5), Escape keeps them, and the `rInit` error ends the room before its `room_end` writes `keys.cfg`/`gamepad.cfg`; without a joystick, `rJoyConfig` only counts Escapes. | `objects/oKeyConfig/keypress_anykey.gml:54`, `objects/oJoyConfig/step.gml:67`, `objects/oOKButton/mouse_left_release.gml:3` | GML | `crates/game/tests/config_screens.rs` |
| The settings program (`config.gmk`, ported as `crates/config`): its key config asks for eleven keys, with no flare (`status` 0-10, pay last), so `keys.cfg`'s flare line keeps its old value; with the doubled "any key" press (§5), six presses bind them all. Enter quits **without** saving (`oInit`'s Enter press is a bare `game_end()`), while Escape and the close button save first. | `config.gmk`: `objects/oKeyConfig/keypress_anykey.gml`, `objects/oInit/keypress_enter.gml` | GML | `crates/config/tests/settings.rs`, `fidelity/settings/key-config.txt` |
| **The flare key is read but never checked.** `scrInit` reads `global.keyFlareVal` (F by default, or `keys.cfg`'s flare line) and `global.joyFlareVal` (`gamepad.cfg`'s), and `oGamepad`'s step polls the flare button, but `oPlayer1`'s step has `// kFlarePressed = checkFlarePressed();`, and the code that would light a flare from it is commented out too (`global.flares`, "not used any more", is never read). So the key does nothing in play. The settings program's side, which never asks for the key, is the row above. It's reached in normal play, by pressing F in a level. | `scripts/scrInit.gml:48,57,113,134`, `scripts/checkFlarePressed.gml`, `objects/oGamepad/step.gml:366-405`, `objects/oPlayer1/step.gml:413,1321-1358`, `scripts/scrClearGlobals.gml:115` | GML + Oracle | `config-keys-tutorial.txt` (F pressed in `rTutorial`, 1070, with no `oFlare` after; the oracle's trace is the same as without the press) |
| **The settings program never reads DOWN KEY TO RUN.** `oInit` starts `global.downToRun` false, where the game's `scrInit` starts it true, and reads the file's line only as `if (str == "0") global.downToRun = false;`, so a saved 1 is lost: the box always comes up unchecked, and every save (OK, Escape, the close button, KEY CONFIG and JOY CONFIG) writes 0 back unless it's checked again. Only the program writes `settings.cfg`, so the game runs with the setting on only after it's checked and saved in that session (or with no file, or one edited by hand). Low: a corner of the settings program. | `config.gmk`: `objects/oInit/create.gml:9,48-49`, `scripts/scrSaveSettings.gml:29-30`; the game: `scripts/scrInit.gml:32,70-71` | GML + Oracle | `fidelity/settings/down-to-run-not-read.txt` (a file with it on: `downToRun` never 1, the box unchecked, and Escape saves it off); the game's side: `config-volumes-top-notches.txt` (the same file's 1 read as on) |
| **The volumes' top notches do nothing in the game.** The settings program's sliders go from 2 to 19 (a slider's volume is its `x / 8`, from 16 to 152, and `oInit` clamps the file's volumes to the same range), but the game's `scrInit` clamps them to 0 to 17. So 17, 18 and 19 are the same volume in the game, and no saved file gives the game's quietest two, 0 and 1. Low: a corner of the settings program. | `config.gmk`: `objects/oInit/create.gml:63-66`, `objects/oSlider1/step.gml:1-16`, `objects/oSlider2/step.gml:1-16`; the game: `scripts/scrInit.gml:85-88` | GML + Oracle | `fidelity/settings/sound-joy-exit.txt` (the sound slider dragged to 18 and saved), `fidelity/settings/escape-saves.txt` (a file's 30 read as 19 and saved); the game's side: `config-volumes-top-notches.txt` (a file's 19 and 18 read as 17) |

| No alien craft or Yeti lair until the Moai is made: they're the `else if` branches of the Moai's `if (not global.madeMoai and global.levelType == 2)`, which every Ice Caves level takes until one rolls the Moai. Level 9 never has either (it has the Moai with 1 in 4), and since the Moai is certain by level 12, they appear only on the levels after it. None of 300 seeds of `fidelity/gen-area.sh ice` has either on level 9; `ice-seed-*.txt` show them on later levels, which the oracle matches. The lair's roll also tests `not global.alienCraft`, which always holds there: the roll is the `else if` after the craft's, and `scrLevelGen` clears `alienCraft` at its start. No effect. | `scripts/scrLevelGen.gml:37,213-237` | GML + Oracle | `ice-seed-*.txt` |
| A dark level needs the previous level's clock to read more than 20 *seconds past the minute*: `oTransition` sets `global.noDarkLevel` ("speed run") from `floor(xtime / 1000)` minus whole minutes, so a level that took 1:05 blocks a dark level just like one that took 0:05. The clock adds `room_speed` (30) per step, so a "second" is 33.3 frames. `scrClearGlobals` starts a game with `noDarkLevel` true, so the first level after the title, including the shortcut levels 5, 9 and 13, is never dark. Only then does `scrInitLevel`'s `rand(1, probDarkLevel)` (1 in 12) count, though it's drawn on every level (§2). **The Black Market's level rolls too, and a dark roll there uses up the area's dark level:** the roll sets `global.hadDarkLevel` before `if (global.blackMarket) global.darkLevel = false;` keeps the market lit, and `hadDarkLevel` is cleared only on levels 1, 5, 9 and 13, so no later Jungle level is dark. That's reached 1 time in 12 on a market on level 6 or 7 (one on level 8 has no Jungle level after it), when the level before it took more than 20 seconds past the minute and the area has had no dark level. | `objects/oTransition/draw.gml:89-102`, `objects/oGame/step.gml:18-19`, `scripts/scrInitLevel.gml:31-37,167-176` | GML + Oracle | `trap-dark-spear.txt`, `trap-dark-arrow.txt`, `trap-dark-smash.txt`; `jungle-seed-9-market-dark-roll.txt` (the market's roll is 1: `hadDarkLevel` set and the market lit, 1673; level 7's roll is 1 too, with `noDarkLevel` clear, and it isn't dark) |

A damsel's `status` is never 98 (stunned), so her `favor*1.5` sacrifice branches are dead: her sacrifice adds 8 favor, never 12 (or nothing at -8 and below, when Kali devours it). | `objects/oLaser/collision_oSolid.gml`, `scripts/scrInitLevel.gml:231`, `objects/oPlayer1/step.gml:2001`, `scripts/scrRoomGen2.gml:350`, `objects/oDamsel/step.gml:331,337` | GML | `fidelity/replays/terrain-kali-sacrifice.txt` (favor 0 -> 8) |
| `persistent` is set on the parent objects `oCharacter` and `oEnemy`, which are never instantiated; children don't inherit the flag, so it has no effect. | object properties | GML; inheritance rule Assumed | test TODO |
| Whether stealing a ware equips it depends on which leaves the shop's room first. `oPlayer1`'s step equips a held ware whose `cost` is above 0 once the player is outside the shop (`scrStealItem`); `oItem`'s step only angers the shopkeeper and sets `cost` to 0 once the *item* is outside. The item is held 4 px ahead of the player, so running out of a shop forwards, the item can cross first and the player is left holding a free, unequipped item, which down + X (drop and pick up again) then equips. But the item tests its position before it follows the player, so it tests where its previous step put it, while the player tests his after moving. Walking (3 px a frame), the item tests a point 1 px ahead of him, so it's out first only when a step leaves him 1 px short of leaving the room; otherwise both are out on the same step, and `oPlayer1` (object 319) steps before every ware, so he takes it and it's equipped at once. A player faster than 4 px a frame (the run key's 6) is always out first. Jumping out upwards, the player crosses first and it's equipped at once. A weapon has nothing to equip, only its message ("YOU GOT A PISTOL!"), and `scrStealItem` shows a weapon's only while its `cost` is above 0, so a weapon out first is taken with none, then or when it's picked up again (GML: no replay steals a weapon this way). The Ankh is the exception: it's never for sale (its row below), so the player is never left holding it free and unequipped. The Kissing Parlor's Damsel is stolen by the same order, which also decides whether she's counted bought or stolen (her row below). | `objects/oPlayer1/step.gml:541-557`, `objects/oItem/step.gml:18-42`, `objects/oPlayer1/step.gml:1297`, `scripts/scrStealItem.gml:243-312` | GML + Oracle | `fidelity/replays/item-seed-7-spike-stomp.txt` (held, then equipped), `item-seed-9-ankh.txt` (equipped at once, though the Ankh always is: its row below), `item-seed-35-cape.txt` (running out on the ground, equipped at once), `item-seed-35-jetpack.txt` (the same, in the air) |
| The Hedjet (`oCrown`) is sealed in every Ice Caves Moai: it sits by the Moai's exit inside `oMoaiInside`, an invincible 16x48 solid that fills the statue's inside, which nothing but the ankh's revive destroys (putting the player in there). So the crown takes dying with the ankh on a Moai level, or a teleporter jump into the chamber (851-3227). The ankh is sold only in the Black Market (levels 6-8), so the player has to carry it, stolen or for $50000, through to level 9 or later without dying. The revive puts the player at the Moai's x+24, y+24, stunned, and he falls onto the crown; picking it up (down + X) runs `scrStealItem`, as for any held item whose `cost` is 0 (the Moai's crown's is), which sets `global.hasCrown` and destroys it. The Moai's own `oExit` (`type` "Moai Exit") then takes him on. That `type` matters: `scrInitLevel` puts an angry Shopkeeper at each exit whose `type` is "Exit" when the player is a thief or a murderer, so none is ever made in the sealed chamber. | `scripts/scrRoomGen3.gml:487-500`, `scripts/scrInitLevel.gml:245-257`, `objects/oPlayer1/step.gml:1853-1861`, `scripts/scrRoomGenMarket.gml:111-115`, `objects/oPlayer1/step.gml:1297`, `scripts/scrStealItem.gml:46-56` | GML + Oracle | `city-of-gold-seed-9.txt` (the ankh carried from the Black Market to level 12's Moai); `item-seed-9-ankh.txt` revives at the entrance |
| The gold door can be sealed in rock. `scrEntityGen` puts it on level 14 above the first block with a free tile above it that its `rand(1, goldChance)` picks (or, failing that, the first such block), anywhere in the level, so it can sit in a pocket no way leads into, which only breaking its rock opens: an explosion (a bomb's, on the screen or off it) or the Mattock's hit, which both destroy any solid but an `invincible` one (the level's border, the block under the door), and the rest of the pocket's rock is ordinary. Nor does it skip Kali's altar, as the Spear Traps' roll beside it does (`type != "Altar"`), so the door can stand on either half of one, and that half gets the door's `invincible = true`: explosions and the Mattock spare it. The other half's destroy event still destroys both (`with oSacAltarLeft`, which takes in its child `oSacAltarRight`), and the door's half still crumbles when the floor under it goes (once it's within the view, as every altar half does), so the altar goes whenever the other half or that floor does. It's reached: a sweep of 3,757 level 14s, rerolled by waiting at level 13's exit, put the door on the altar in 15, 1 in 77 of the 1,156 with an altar. The door takes the sceptre (`collision_oSceptre` destroys it) and turns into `oXGold`, so the sceptre never reaches the City of Gold. A tomb lord that walks into lava dies there: the `oEnemy` step it inherits finds its feet in `oLava` and sets its `hp` to 0 with `countsAsKill` off (so neither `global.kills`, `global.tomblords` nor the kill table counts it), and its own step then drops the sceptre into the lava. (One with its head in lava too is destroyed outright by the line before, with no sceptre.) While in view, `oItem`'s step sinks the sceptre 0.05 px a step there and, though it burns up any other item whose top goes under, spares the sceptre. | `scripts/scrEntityGen.gml:385-410,480-495`, `objects/oExplosion/collision_oSolid.gml:1-14`, `objects/oMattockHit/other_animation_end.gml:1-22`, `objects/oSacAltarLeft/destroy.gml:62-66`, `objects/oSacAltarLeft/step.gml:1-5`, `objects/oGoldDoor/collision_oSceptre.gml`, `objects/oEnemy/step.gml:63-74`, `objects/oTombLord/step.gml:16-30`, `objects/oItem/step.gml:160-172` | GML + Oracle | `city-of-gold-seed-9-sealed-door.txt` (level 14's door in a pocket two bombs open, which then takes the sceptre; no replay opens one with the Mattock, whose hit tests the same `invincible` as the explosion (`item-seed-35-mattock-break.txt` digs a tunnel of ordinary blocks)), `temple-seed-113.txt` (level 13's tomb lord walks into lava and drops the sceptre there while the player stands still); `city-of-gold-seed-9.txt` (a door in the open); `temple-seed-1-gold-door-on-altar.txt` (level 14, rerolled: the door on the altar's left half, which is `invincible`, 1328, and the player on the right half beside it, 1435); wiki clip `quirks/gold-door-on-altar` |
| **On level 14, no block visited before the gold door's becomes a Spear Trap.** `scrEntityGen`'s door branch is the `if` of the Spear Traps' `else if`, and until the door is made it takes every block with `y` over 32 and a free tile above it. Every Spear Trap candidate is such a block (it needs free tiles 16 and 32 px above it, and the level's border fills `y` 0), so each one before the door's block rolls the door's `rand(1, goldChance)` instead of the Spear Traps' `rand(1,12)`. `goldChance` starts at 160 (`scrClearGlobals`) and drops by one with each failed roll, so the door lands on each of the first 160 such blocks 1 time in 160, after 80 of them on average. The fallback (the first such block) needs a level with fewer than 160, and none of a sweep's 3,757 level 14s used it. It's reached on every level 14: in the sweep, a third of the blocks that qualify (7 of 21 on average) came before the door, and its level 14s averaged 1.0 Spear Traps. | `scripts/scrEntityGen.gml:399-442`, `scripts/scrClearGlobals.gml:80` | GML + Oracle | `city-of-gold-seed-2-sceptre.txt` (level 14's door on the 125th such block, `goldChance` 36 at 1446; 23 blocks before it qualify, none of the 9 after it rolls a trap, and the level has no Spear Trap), `temple-seed-1.txt` (the door on the 24th, `goldChance` 137 at 1130; 7 qualify before it, and the level's two Spear Traps come after) |
| The locked chest's key can be sealed in rock. A level that makes the Udjat eye's locked chest (`global.genUdjatEye`: level 2 one time in 3, level 3 one in 2, else level 4, until one is made) turns one of its `oTreasure` that isn't inside a solid into the key: `scrEntityGen` tries each with `rand(1,8)` for up to 7 passes, then takes the first. The treasure can be anywhere in the level, so the key can sit in a pocket no way leads into, the chest sometimes with it, which only breaking its rock opens, as with the gold door's pocket (the idol trap's boulder breaks blocks too, but only those in its roll's way). The chest only opens to the key held (`other.held`), so the key has to be carried to it. | `scripts/scrInitLevel.gml:178-184`, `scripts/scrEntityGen.gml:108-143`, `objects/oExplosion/collision_oSolid.gml:1-14`, `objects/oMattockHit/other_animation_end.gml:1-22`, `objects/oBoulder/collision_oSolid.gml:1-22`, `objects/oLockedChest/collision_oKey.gml` | GML + Oracle | `item-seed-35-udjat-eye-sealed-key.txt` (level 2's key and chest share a pocket, which two bombs open; the key is crawled to the chest; none opens one with the Mattock, as the gold door's row says) |
| The City of Gold's Lady Xoc room can seal the way to the exit. `scrLevelGen` overwrites a random room of the third row with it (`roomPath` 6) after the path is laid, whatever the path needed there, and the room's bottom row is solid, so when it lands on the path's drop room nothing leads down to the exit: only breaking through the floor does, with a bomb or the Mattock, as with the gold door's pocket. With seed 9 it takes the drop room above the exit's row, so the run bombs through the floor beside the statue. Six of its 30 `oXocBlock`s hold a gem (`treasure`: a diamond, two sapphires, two emeralds, a ruby) and drop two of it when destroyed, and in the City every one drops three gold chunks and a nugget. The statue can take a sacrifice pit's room too. On a pit's level (1 in `probSacPit`, 8, of the Temple's) `scrLevelGen` marks the pit's column (`roomPath` 7, 8, 8, 9, top to bottom) before the path walk, which never enters it, and one time in four the City's line then picks the pit's third room: 1 City in 32. The pit's shaft then ends on the statue's top, a room down, and the pit's bottom, with its lava and Big Ruby blocks, is sealed under the statue room's floor. | **An angry Kali's sacrifice zeroes the body's value after adding it.** In `oEnemy`'s and `oDamsel`'s sacrifice, the branch for a `global.favor` below 0 (but above -8, where Kali devours the body) adds the body's `favor` as the branch for 0 or more does (half of it for a dead monster), then runs `if (favor > 0) favor = 0;` on the body, which is destroyed a few lines on. | **Items freeze outside the view.** `oItem`'s step, which the items without a step of their own inherit (the rock, for one), only runs while the item is within 16 px of the view (or is a rope), so a thrown item that leaves the view stops in mid-air, once past that margin, with its `xVel` and `yVel`, active, before `oLevel` deactivates it 96 px out, and flies on when the view comes back to it. | `objects/oItem/step.gml:1` | GML + oracle | `item-seed-49-rock-off-view.txt` (978-1263); wiki clip `quirks/rock-freezes-off-view` |

| **A fast thrown item hurts the nearest enemy, not the one it hit.** While an item moves faster than 2 px a step, `oItem`'s step looks for an enemy touching it with `collision_rectangle(x-2, y-2, x+2, y+2, oEnemy, 0, 0)`, but then hurts `instance_nearest(x, y, oEnemy)`: the enemy of any kind whose position is nearest the item's (`instance_nearest` measures to positions, not boxes, §5). Most enemies' origin is their sprite's top left, so an item touching one enemy's right side can be nearer the next enemy's position: that one is hurt (it loses `hp`, some kinds are stunned, and its `xVel` becomes 0.3 of the item's) and the one hit isn't. The invincibility and Magma Man test is the nearest one's too, so an invincible enemy or a Magma Man nearest the item spares the one it hit, and nobody is hurt. It's reached in normal play, by any fast item and two enemies close together: a thrown rock, an arrow from a trap, and the jar, the skull and the dice, whose own steps have the same pick (the jar's and the skull's with a ±3 px rectangle, the dice's above 3 px a step), and do it for damsels too (`instance_nearest(x, y, oDamsel)`). A sticky bomb latches onto the nearest enemy the same way, when it latches at all (the precedence row above). A stunned enemy thrown into another (`oEnemy`'s step) hurts the one `collision_rectangle` returns, as do `oItem`'s damsel check and the sticky bomb's damsel latch. | `objects/oItem/step.gml:219-221,256-363`, `objects/oJar/step.gml:106-108,150-152`, `objects/oSkull/step.gml:104-106,148-150`, `objects/oDice/step.gml:110-114,185-187`; `objects/oEnemy/step.gml:174` | GML + Oracle | `action-throw-rock-nearest-enemy.txt` (309: a rock thrown up touches a hanging spider's box, and the bat hanging beside it dies; without the bat the spider dies, without the spider the bat lives a frame longer); wiki clip `quirks/thrown-item-nearest-enemy` |
| **A thrown item pushes the monster it hits with 0.3 of its speed, and a thrown monster with all of its own.** `oItem`'s step sets the monster's `xVel` (the one it hurts, the nearest: the row above) to the item's, `obj.xVel = xVel`, before the hit, and to `xVel * 0.3` after it, so the first assignment is dead: nothing between reads it. `oEnemy`'s step, for a stunned monster (`status` 98) moving faster than 2 px a step into another, has the same first assignment, as `if (obj.status < 98) obj.xVel = xVel;`, and its 0.3 line commented out (`// obj.xVel = xVel * 0.3;`). | **Picking up a stunned enemy takes the nearest enemy, not the one under the player.** Crouched (down) with empty hands, X looks for an enemy at the player's feet with `collision_rectangle(x-8, y, x+8, y+8, oEnemy, 0, 0)`, but then takes `instance_nearest(x, y, oEnemy)`: the enemy of any kind whose position is nearest the player's (§5), and picks it up only if *that one* is stunned or dead (`status >= 98`) and `canPickUp`. Most enemies' origin is their sprite's top left (the caveman's and the yeti's among them), so a player over one enemy's right side can be nearer the next enemy's position: he lifts that one if it's stunned, though it isn't at his feet, and nothing if it isn't (a live enemy beside a stunned one keeps it on the ground), and the one under him stays where it lies. It's reached in normal play, by two enemies close together, one of them stunned: a stomped caveman or yeti beside another enemy. Items have the same pick and come first: `collision_rectangle(x-8, y, x+8, y+8, oItem, 0, 0)`, then `instance_nearest(x, y, oItem)`, which must be `canPickUp` with its position out of solids, so the item lifted is the nearest one, any item (the damsel is an `oItem`) or none, and while any item touches the rectangle no enemy is tried. Collecting treasure (`instance_nearest(x, y, oTreasure)`, if it `canCollect`) and the bow taking back an arrow (`oArrow`) pick the nearest the same way, so the treasure collected can be a nearer one the player isn't touching (the next row); the bomb bag, the bomb box and the rope pile use `collision_rectangle`'s result. | `objects/oPlayer1/step.gml:1176-1209,1300-1309,1977-1980,1990-1993` | GML + Oracle | `action-pickup-nearest-enemy.txt` (445: crouched over a stunned yeti's right side, the player lifts the stunned caveman beside it; without the yeti nothing is lifted, without the caveman the yeti is); wiki clip `quirks/pickup-nearest-enemy` |
| **Collecting treasure takes the nearest treasure, and nothing while that one can't be collected.** `oPlayer1`'s step checks that a treasure touches the player (`collision_rectangle(x-8, y-8, x+8, y+8, oTreasure, 0, 0)`) but then takes `instance_nearest(x, y, oTreasure)` (§5), touching him or not, and collects it only if *that one* `canCollect`. | **The Moon Room's goal shows for any winning time.** `oHighscores`' create sets `moonGold = 600000`, and its draw, while the player is beside the trophies at the Moon Room's height (`oPlayer1.y` at least 64 and under 96), shows the room's goal, SHOOT THE MOVING TARGETS WITH YOUR BOW AND ARROWS!, rather than LOCKED., `if (tTime > 0 and tTime <= moonGold)`. But `tTime` is the best time in seconds (`scrUpdateHighscores` divides the clock by 1000 before storing it), and the door itself (`oXMoon`) needs `tTime <= 600`, so with a best time over 10:00 the panel shows the goal of a room with no door. The Sun Room's `sunGold` (200000) and the Stars Room's `starsGold` (120) match their doors. | **A target stomped in the Moon Room's last 20 seconds comes back where it had swayed to, worth 3.** An arrow's hit makes the replacement `oBatTarget` at the target's `xMid`, `instance_create(xMid, -64, oBatTarget)`, but landing on it makes it at `x`. From `oMoonRoom.timer <= 20` the target's step sways it, `x = xMid - abs(sin(xDiff)*32)`, while its `y` is over 64, and never puts it back, so a target stomped once it has started to sway comes back at the swayed `x`, up to 32 px left of its path. `oBatTarget`'s create tests `x == 208` and `x == 256` for the middle and far targets (worth 2 and 4), misses, and makes the replacement worth 1, 3 as a UFO, whatever it replaced, swaying round its new `xMid`, further left. So the middle and far targets come back worth 4 and 6 when shot, but 3 when stomped; the near one, worth 3 either way, only moves. | **A win from a shortcut shows TIME: 0:00.** `scrUpdateHighscores(1)` keeps a win's time only `not global.usedShortcut`, which the title's level 5, 9 and 13 shortcut doors set for the whole game (its start and tutorial doors clear it), so a shortcut win leaves the table's `TIME` as it was: 0, until a win from the start or tutorial door. | **Playing as the Damsel, the title rope's climb out shows the Spelunker.** Climbing the title screen's rope to the top (`y < 32`) is the way out: `oPlayer1`'s step replaces the player with `oPDummy5`, which climbs on out of sight before the fade to `rCredits1`. | **On a first run, the title screen has no hint hand after its opening.** With `global.firstTime` (set by `oGlobals` while the highscore table holds none of the game's entries, as on the very first run: every entry at or below a starting value, which the table `scrResetHighscores` then writes fails, its `TUNNEL1` and `TUNNEL2` being above theirs; the START door clears it on the way into the Tutorial), `oTitle`'s create makes `oHintHand`, which points at the START door and draws PRESS UP TO ENTER! while the player stands within 8 px of it, only in its `titleStart` 1, 2 and 3 branches: back from the high scores room; the opening skipped, or back from the level editor or the load-level menu; back from the Tutorial. The opening's branch, `titleStart == 0`, makes none, and the opening ends with `oPDummy4` making the player, without the room starting again. So a new player who watches the opening through gets no hand, while one who skips it (Enter, Escape, attack or start while `oScreen.canPause` is false sets `titleStart = 2` and restarts the room) gets one, as does one who goes into the scores door and comes back. The `titleStart` 3 branch's hand never shows: on a first run the tutorial door is bricked over, and the START door clears `firstTime`. Possibly by design. | **A saved level without exits loses its sign messages.** `keypress_Y` and `scrTestLevel` only end the exit-count line `if (exitNamesID > 0)`, so with no exit or entrance names the sign count goes on the same line: `0` and `1` make `01`. `scrLoadLevel` and the editor then read as many exit names as there are signs (the signs' messages, given to the exits and entrances, if any) and no sign messages, so the signs come back with their default text. With exits, the names are each followed by a line end (`if (i < exitNamesID)` is always true), which the loader's `readln` before the sign count expects. `keypress_Y` also calls `file_delete(levelName)` on the bare name, not the `levels/` path, which deletes nothing. | `oLevelEditor` `keypress_Y`, `scrTestLevel`, `scrLoadLevel` | GML + oracle (the `.lvl` files the editor replays write match byte for byte) | `editor-sign-no-exit.txt`, `editor-sign-no-exit-reopen.txt` (the saved level opened again in the editor: the sign's `message` is `HELLO` on 226 and empty on 286); wiki clip `quirks/sign-message-lost` |
| **Pushing a block into a wall crushes the player.** `moveTo` moves the player a pixel at a time, and for a moveable solid in the way runs `with (solidId) { if place_meeting(x+1, y, oSolid) break; else x += 1; }`. GM8's `break` inside `with` leaves only the `with` (§2), so when the block is stuck the player's `for` goes on and steps into it anyway. `characterStepEvent` keeps him pushing (`colSolidRight` only looks for an `oMoveableSolid`, which the line through the block still finds), so he creeps in at the pushing speed (`xVel` 0.6, a pixel every other frame) until his centre is inside the block and `oPlayer1`'s step kills him as crushed (`collision_point(x, y, oSolid)`, `plife -= 99`). It's the same pushing any block against any solid, button or not. In `rHighscores` the block's only stop is `oResetSign` (an `oSolid`, x 240), right past the reset button (`oButtonHighscore`, x 224), so a player who keeps pushing it there is crushed ~10 frames after it stops. There it isn't a death: the branch that sets `dead`, records the stats and ends the game only runs in a level or a bonus room, and the room's own guard puts `plife` back to 4 on the next step. The player just stays invisible, with `active` false, and no game over follows. | `scripts/moveTo.gml:39-65` (left: `:67-93`), `scripts/characterStepEvent.gml:106-107,158-168`, `objects/oPlayer1/step.gml:1-7,1490-1511,1849-1919` | GML + Oracle | `flow-highscores-reset-crush.txt` (crushed at 845); wiki clip `quirks/highscores-block-crush` |

| **One caveman's sight sets off every caveman and hawkman near the player.** Cavemen, hawkmen and yetis look for the player by sending an `oEnemySight` the way they face every 6 steps (10 px a step; the first solid it touches destroys it). That's whoever sent it, whichever way each one faces, whether or not it could see him: one walled off from him charges, and one facing away charges away from him (a charge runs the way the enemy faces). The one that saw him charges only if it's within those 100 px too, so a caveman that sees the player from farther away walks on. A yeti's sight sets them off as well, but never a yeti: there's no `with oYeti`. The sight's `owner` (`instance_place(x, y, oCaveman)`, `oHawkman` or `oYeti`: another instance of the sender's object that it overlaps, usually `noone`) is never read. | **A caveman slides off the ledge it stops at.** Walking, it checks for floor past its front edge, and where there's none sets `status = IDLE` and `xVel = 0`, but then sets `xVel` to ±1.5 again in the same step (the check comes first). So it stops at the edge still moving, and its friction (0.1 a step, to 0 below 0.5) carries it about 10 px on: past the edge, but not off it. The next time it sets off that way it stops at once and slides again, off the edge this time, and falls. The rest have 1, and die to the first step (a hanging giant spider, 10, becomes an `oGiantSpider` once it's hurt, whose whip waits and whose wind-up doesn't). | **The Whip hurts the Yeti King only with its wind-up.** `oYetiKing`'s collision with the whip (`oWhip`, the crack in front of the player) only hurts it if the hit punctures, which the Whip's (`puncture = false`) doesn't, so the crack only plays the hit sound (and only reaches its top 12 px: `other.y < y+12`). But it has no collision with the wind-up behind the player (`oWhipPre`) of its own, as the yeti has (one that only pushes it back, unless it's the Machete's), so it takes `oEnemy`'s: the wind-up's `damage` (1) on each of its 3 steps it touches it, with no pause, so a swing with the player's back to it takes 3 (or facing it, from inside its box). | **The Pitcher's Mitt's light gravity lasts one step, as an arrow's does, but a jar, a skull or a die keeps it.** A throw from the hands with the Mitt (not crouching) sets the item's `myGrav` to 0.1, a sixth of the usual 0.6. But `oItem`'s step, which most items run, puts `myGrav` back to 0.6 on every step the item isn't in lava, after that step's gravity: a Rock, a held bomb or any other such item falls at 0.1 for its first step and 0.6 from the next, so a level throw from the ground (0.4 up) is on the floor within 4 steps and stops nearer than one without the Mitt. A jar, a skull and a die have steps of their own instead of `oItem`'s, which set `myGrav` only in lava (to 0), so they keep the 0.1 for good: thrown level, a jar flies flat across a room, and it falls slowly ever after, as does a die thrown in a Craps shop. Monsters and the damsel set their gravity again on every step (`oEnemy`'s and `oDamsel`'s water checks), so they lose it within a step too. An arrow's own gravity goes the same way: `oArrow`'s create event sets `myGrav = 0.2`, a third of the usual, but its step runs `oItem`'s first, so every arrow, a Bow's or an arrow trap's, falls at 0.2 for its first step and at 0.6 from the next, as fast as a thrown Rock. It's reached by every throw with the Mitt, and by every arrow. | `scripts/scrUseItem.gml:667-676`, `objects/oItem/step.gml:84,160-170`, `objects/oArrow/create.gml:6`, `objects/oArrow/step.gml:1`, `objects/oJar/step.gml:1,27-30,81-86`, `objects/oSkull/step.gml:1,27-30,79-84`, `objects/oDice/step.gml:54,94-99`, `objects/oEnemy/step.gml:42,54`, `objects/oDamsel/step.gml:42,47` | GML + Oracle | `item-mitt-gravity-one-step.txt` (a Rock thrown level falls at 0.1, yVel -0.3 on 301, then 0.6, +0.3 on 302, and is on the floor by 305; a Jar thrown the same way keeps 0.1, -0.3 to 0.8 a step at a time, 361-372, lands on 373 and still has it); wiki clip `quirks/mitt-gravity-one-step`; `weapon-bow-bomb-arrow-free.txt` (a Bow's arrow shot level, 282, has a `yVel` of 0.2, then 0.8, 1.4 and 2, 282-285) |
| **The Pitcher's Mitt's catch holds every Rock.** A Rock moving faster than 4 px a step across that touches the player (`instance_nearest`, once its 10 `safe` steps after a throw are over) costs him 2 hearts and stuns him. So `holdItem` is `oRock`'s object index (450), and `held` is set on every active Rock, whose `oItem` steps then put them all in his hands: every Rock near the view flies to him, the ones lying on the floor too, and stays there while he holds anything. What reads `holdItem.x` and the like reads the first Rock. Throwing them sets every Rock's `held`, `safe`, `xVel` and `yVel` alike (`scrUseItem`'s `holdItem.xVel = ...`), so they all leave together, one on top of the other. | **`scrUseItem`'s last line moves the held item to the player's `y` on every use, and writes to object 0 after a throw.** The line is `if (kDown and holdItem) holdItem.x = x; holdItem.y = y;`, and only the first assignment is the `if`'s, so `holdItem.y = y` runs on every use of a held item (X), crouched or not. After a throw (a weapon's crouched throw too) `holdItem` is 0 by then, so it sets `y` on object 0's instances, and the game has none (its objects are numbered from 126, as the `distance_to_object` row says): nothing happens. After any other use he still holds an item (a gun, the Bow, the Web Cannon or the Sceptre fired, the Machete or the Mattock swung, the Teleporter used, a bomb lit, or what he held before taking out a bomb or a rope that he's just thrown, which `scrHoldItem` makes again at his `x` and `y`), and it's moved to his `y`. That lasts only until the item's own step: a step runs object by object, in index order (§5), `oPlayer1` is object 319 and every item 450 or more, and `oItem`'s held code, which each of them runs first, puts it back beside him (his `y` + 2, + 4 crouched) before anything draws it. The one exception is a Teleporter that takes him far up. `oItem`'s step runs only for an item within 16 px of the view, which follows him only when it's drawn, keeping him at least 96 px from its top (`view_vborder`). A teleport up picks 4 to 8 blocks (64 to 128 px), and more to get out of a solid, so one of 8 blocks or more from close to that border, as a second teleport soon after a first is, can leave him, and the Teleporter with him, more than 16 px above the view: its held code is skipped, and it's drawn level with him, 2 px higher than on every other frame, for that frame. | **Stomping a Yeti, the Yeti King or a Shopkeeper on the way up takes nothing.** Their stomps take `ceil(fallTimer/16)` hit points (3 times that in Spike Shoes), where `oEnemy`'s and the other monsters' take `floor(fallTimer/16)+1`. `fallTimer` counts the player's steps of falling, and his step sets it to 0 on every step he isn't falling (`yVel` 0 or less), while a stomp needs only `JUMPING` or `FALLING` (the row on stomps above). So a stomp while he's still rising, jumping into one with his feet above its top 5 px (the Yeti King's 8), takes 0: the Yeti and the Shopkeeper are stunned all the same, and he bounces off. | **The Mattock's hit can break the block under the player, or the one beyond the block ahead.** When its animation ends, `oMattockHit`, 16 px ahead of the player at his height, tests the point at its position (its `x` and `y`) for a solid, and if there's none, the point 9 px below it, in the block ahead of his feet. But the block it breaks is the one `instance_place` finds there, which tests the hit's whole mask (`sMattockHitR`'s box is x-8 to x+2, `sMattockHitL`'s x-3 to x+7) and returns the first block in instance order that overlaps it, not necessarily the one at the point. Facing right with the player at x ≡ 0 to 7 (mod 16), the mask also overlaps the block he stands on, and facing left at x ≡ 0 to 2, the block beyond the one ahead; where that block comes first, it breaks instead, and he drops through the floor, or digs past a block that stays (at his height too, against a wall). A custom level makes its blocks row by row from the left, so there the left block of the two comes first: facing right, at half of all positions the Mattock digs the player's own floor. | **A bomb arrow costs no bomb, and the bomb key only readies one at full draw.** With the Bow drawn (`bowArmed`), the bomb key readies a bomb arrow (`holdArrow = ARROW_BOMB`) `if (global.bombs > 0)`, but nothing takes the bomb: not that line, not `scrFireBow`, which gives the arrow what's left of its counter (`alarm[1] = bombArrowCounter`), nor its explosion. A bomb thrown the usual way takes one (`global.bombs -= 1`). So with one bomb, the player has as many bomb arrows as arrows. But the draw, later in the same step, sets `holdArrow = ARROW_NORM` on every step it draws (`bowStrength < 12`, 60 steps of 0.2), so the key does nothing until the Bow is fully drawn. | **The Teleporter keeps its last spot while another weapon's shot waits, and with none it stops the game.** `scrUseItem` picks the Teleporter's spot, `tx` and `ty`, with up held, or else facing left or right `and firing == 0`. `firing` is the wait a shot starts, which `oPlayer1`'s step counts down: the Pistol's, the Web Cannon's and the Sceptre's 20 steps, the Shotgun's 40, the Bow's 10. The Teleporter starts none, but a player who puts a gun down and picks a Teleporter up inside its wait, and uses it sideways (not crouching, up not held), keeps `tx` and `ty` as they were last set, and is moved there, wherever he is. They're `oPlayer1`'s own variables, and two scripts besides write them in his scope as they go: `isInShop` and `scrGetRoomX`/`scrGetRoomY` set them to the point they're asked about, and his step asks about his own position on every step he holds a ware with a price on a level or a readied bomb arrow, and when he presses the pay key on a level. So the spot is the last of these: the Teleporter's last use, or where he last held an unpaid ware, say. If none has set them since the level's `oPlayer1` was made, the use reads the unset `tx`, an error (the game is built with "treat uninitialized variables as 0" off), which stops the game: a Teleporter carried in from the level before or found, and used this way before any of the others. It's reached by swapping a gun or the Bow for a Teleporter and using it within the wait. | `scripts/scrUseItem.gml:426-458`, `objects/oPlayer1/step.gml:175`, `objects/oPlayer1/create.gml:51-54`, `scripts/isInShop.gml:7-8`, `scripts/scrGetRoomX.gml:24`, `scripts/scrGetRoomY.gml:24`, `objects/oPlayer1/step.gml:43,543-545,1359-1361,1392`; `scripts/scrUseItem.gml:258,273,316,329,377,395,574,592`, `scripts/scrFireBow.gml:39,52` | GML + Oracle | `weapon-teleporter-old-target.txt` (used with up held, 272, it moves the player 4 blocks up, to (153, 136); with `firing` 6 after a Shotgun shot, used facing left, 405, it moves him up there again; once the wait is over, 441, 5 blocks left); wiki clip `quirks/teleporter-old-target`. The same scene without the first use stops on 405 on the port and the oracle alike, "unknown variable tx" (`scrUseItem.gml:449`), and isn't committed: a replay that stops on an error has no fingerprint. |

| **The Ankh is never marked for sale.** `scrRoomGenMarket` makes the Ankh shop's Ankh itself, with the `cost` of 50000 its create event gives it, but never sets `forSale`, which `scrShopItemsGen` sets on every other ware. So `oItem`'s step, which angers the shopkeeper and sets the `cost` to 0 once a for-sale ware is outside the shop, never acts on it: thrown out of the shop, it lies outside at its price and the shopkeeper stays calm. Only `oPlayer1`'s step acts on it, taking a held ware whose `cost` is above 0 once the player is outside: picking it up there, like carrying it out, puts it on (`scrStealItem`) and angers him. So the row above on stealing never leaves the player with a free, unequipped Ankh. Nor can the Ankh shop be left forwards: the market's layout is fixed, and the Ankh shop's room, (3, 2), opens only at the top, as the casino's solid side closes its left, the level's edge its right, and four rows of brick its floor. Without the Ankh he's dead, and every active Jetpack on the level is gone, behind the game-over screen: in the same step after a crush or the hearts, as `oPlayer1` (object 319) steps before `oJetpack` (738; [runtime.md](design/runtime.md), holders), and in the next after a Man Trap or the Ghost, whose collisions come after the steps. With the Ankh it never happens: after a crush or the hearts, the revival (`visible = true`) comes later in the same step that hid him, and after a Man Trap or the Ghost, his next step revives him before any Jetpack's runs. | **The path walk's `else`s belong to the inner `if`s, so a move toward a room already on the path goes the other way, or down.** `scrLevelGen` lays the path by rolling, from each room, a move left, right or down. A move left is `if (roomX > 0) if (roomPath[roomX-1, roomY] == 0) roomX -= 1; else if (roomX < 3) if (roomPath[roomX+1, roomY] == 0) roomX += 1; else n = 5;`, and a move right its mirror. The indentation pairs each `else` with an edge check, `if (roomX > 0)` and `if (roomX < 3)`: read that way they never run (a move left is only rolled away from the left edge, a move right away from the right), and a move toward a room the path has taken would do nothing and be rolled again. But each `else` belongs to the nearest `if`, so the move goes the other way instead, if that room is free, or down to the next row if both are taken (beside the Temple's sacrificial pit). Only at an edge does a blocked move do nothing. So the path runs on across a row more than the indentation's walk would. | **The big piece of dark rubble falls through the rock.** A block of the Ice Caves' dark rock (`oDark`) or of the mothership (`oAlienShip`) leaves one big piece of rubble (`oRubbleDark`) and two small ones (`oRubbleDarkSmall`), and a falling platform (`oDarkFall`) that lands breaks into three big pieces. The big piece's parent is `oDrawnSprite`, and its own step destroys it only on a brick or a stone block (`collision_point(x, y, oBrick, 0, 0) or collision_point(x, y, oBlock, 0, 0)`), which no Ice Caves level has. Leaving a level, that animation's room change is then pending, so no more events run in the room (the title's character-change doors change no room, but the title has no hull), and the flag stays set until the next level's `scrEntityGen` ends, and nothing in that generation breaks a block. | `objects/oRubbleDark/step.gml:1-8`, `objects/oRubbleDark/other_outside.gml`, `objects/oDarkFall/collision_oSolid.gml:3-8`, `objects/oLevel/step.gml:75`; compare `objects/oRubblePiece/step.gml:16-19`. The forced pass needs every roll to miss, which takes a level with fewer than 160 blocks with a solid above (about (160 - N)/160 for N of them, all able to hold the door), or with none able to from the 160th on. But `oTransition`'s create empties both lines, and every message that sets them again sets the timer too, so what `oLevel` draws last then is nothing. | **Hitting a Shopkeeper frees the wares without marking the player a thief.** The Whip's collision (and the Machete's slash, an `oWhip`), a thrown item's or a thrown body's hit and a Piranha's bite set his `status` to `ATTACK` at once; a bullet or a pellet stuns him (98), and he gets up in `ATTACK`; one squeezed between blocks while he stands by his wares attacks too. None calls `scrShopkeeperAnger`, so there's no shout and `thiefLevel` stays 0. But `ATTACK`'s first step frees every active ware (`cost` 0, `forSale` false), so a ware can then be carried out free, as no theft: no Shopkeeper waits at the next level's exit and its shops greet him. He still attacks. Possibly by design. Falling onto Spikes and a Spear Trap's hit set `countsAsKill` false on him, as on any monster, and the other monsters' deaths read it, but his don't: a Shopkeeper the Spikes kill still adds to `kills`, `shopkeepers` and his kill tally (`enemyKills[19]`), and marks the player a murderer. It lasts the rest of the game (`scrClearGlobals` clears it, for a new game): every Shopkeeper patrols and shouts "YOU'LL PAY FOR YOUR CRIMES!", every ware is free (and counted stolen as it comes on screen, the row above), and each level's exit has a Shopkeeper waiting by it. Possibly by design. It's reached in normal play: the replays show a death after the player's own, from the bomb he left, and one the Spikes cause, an angry Shopkeeper chasing a thief falling onto them, which counts though `countsAsKill` is false (a tomb lord the lava kills, `countsAsKill` false too, isn't counted). | `objects/oShopkeeper/step.gml:24-68,479-503`, `objects/oEnemy/step.gml:76-128`, `objects/oCaveman/step.gml:176`, `objects/oTombLord/step.gml:24-29`, `scripts/scrShopkeeperAnger.gml:13`, `scripts/scrInitLevel.gml:245-257` | GML + Oracle (the player's bomb, Spikes); GML (a Spear Trap) | `shop-seed-11-bomb.txt` (the player dead at 1300, his bomb kills the Shopkeeper, `murderer` 1 at 1383); `shop-seed-3-bomb-rope-piles.txt` (the same, 1172 and 1291); `shop-seed-35-spikes-murderer.txt` (a thief, unhurt, lures him into a hole with Spikes in it: his hit points gone at 1928, and `kills` 2 to 3, `shopkeepers` and `enemyKills[19]` 0 to 1, `murderer` 1, on that step); `temple-seed-113.txt` (the tomb lord dead in lava, 786: `tomblords` stays 0) |
| **A stolen Damsel counts as bought, not stolen.** The Kissing Parlor's Damsel is for sale (`forSale`, at three kisses' price), and carried out unpaid she's stolen as a ware is, by whichever step sees itself out of the shop's room first, the player's or hers (the stealing row above: walking, a tie unless she's a step ahead, and the player, object 319, steps before her, 556). If the player's does, `oPlayer1`'s step runs `scrStealItem`, whose Damsel branch adds 1 to `global.damselsBought` (stats.txt's "DAMSELS BOUGHT"), sets her `cost` to 0 and `forSale` false and shows "YOU MUST BE IN LOVE!", which `scrShopkeeperAnger(0)` replaces on the same step with "COME BACK HERE, THIEF!" when it angers him. `itemsStolen` isn't touched. If hers does (`oDamsel` is an `oItem`, and her step runs its step first), `oItem`'s step counts her in `itemsStolen`, angers him and sets her `cost` to 0 (leaving `forSale` set), and nothing counts her bought: the same order that decides whether a stolen ware is equipped. Either way she's free and he attacks. | **A patrolling Shopkeeper's walk lasts one step.** On patrol (`status` 4, `PATROL`), a Shopkeeper stands `rand(20,50)` steps on the ground, then walks (`WALK`) a random way. His walk goes back to patrolling `if (not collision_point(x-1, y, oSolid))` facing left, or `x+16` facing right: when there's no wall in front of his head. The same lines in the Alien's and the Mantrap's walks, where they turn at a ledge, also test `y+16`, the floor in front. In open ground nothing is in front of his head, so every walk ends on its first step. That step still sets `xVel` to 1.5 his way after the test, so he coasts to a halt over ten steps (his friction takes 0.1 a step), stands, and shuffles off again. Every patrolling Shopkeeper does it: the one `scrInitLevel` puts at a level's exit while the player is a thief or a murderer, a shop's Shopkeeper on such a level (his idle step patrols then), and the level editor's `!`. Falling back, at 0.6 a step more, she reaches `yVel` 2.2 as she comes down to the floor, and her step's `if (not held and yVel > 2 and status != THROWN)` makes her `THROWN`. That counts her in `damselsGrabbed` (stats.txt) if nothing has knocked her down before, and she lies stunned where she lands, but a throw from the hands sets `counter` to `stunMax` (120) and this doesn't: her stun is whatever `counter` held, the countdown to her next cry for help (up to 200, which her cry resets), and she runs when it's out. The Kissing Parlor's Damsel never counts it down, so hers is the 200 of her create event, and once it's out she runs off; the whip angers the Shopkeeper too (`forSale`). A Damsel crushed inside a block, or one whose wounds kill her as she lands (`THROWN` with no hit points), also adds 1 to `damselsKilled`, the kills the screen between levels shows, and to `kills`, the KILLS high score. Unreached: an exiting Damsel can't be picked up, as the exit sets her `canPickUp` false on two of its three paths (her own step when she touches it, and the player's when he touches it carrying her), and on the third, up at the exit with her in his hands, he goes through it too. | `objects/oPlayer1/step.gml:1284-1290`; `objects/oDamsel/step.gml:3-24`, `objects/oPlayer1/step.gml:782-805,2068-2111` | GML | none (never reached) |

The screen between levels shows a Damsel in the loot for each, but `oTransition`'s create event makes one kissing Damsel (`oDamselKiss`) `if (global.xdamsels > 0)`, and her room end adds the one heart. ## 2. GML language semantics

The full list, with counts, is in [translation.md](design/translation.md#evidence-gm8-semantics-the-translation-must-reproduce).
These are the ones that read as bugs to a modern eye.

| Quirk | Where | Status | Test |
|---|---|---|---|
| `and`, `or` and `xor` evaluate **both** operands, side effects included (no short-circuit). With Rust's `&&`, the RNG stream, and every later level, diverges. | runner; 2,255 operators, 68 with `rand`/`random` on the right | OpenGMK (`gml/runtime.rs` `Node::Binary`) | [`and_or_evaluate_both_operands`](../crates/transpile/tests/emit.rs) (translation) |
| `and`, `or`, `xor` share **one** precedence level, left-associative; bitwise operators bind tighter than comparisons. | runner; 3 sites change meaning | OpenGMK (`gml-parser` `get_op_precedence`) | [`gm8_precedence`](../crates/gml/tests/parser.rs) |
| A real is true when it is **at least 0.5** (`0.5` true, `0.49` false, NaN false); strings are always false, but `not "x"` is an error. | runner | OpenGMK (`gml/value.rs` `is_truthy`, `Value::not`); confirmed against [GM8](../tools/gm8-probe/README.md) for 0.5 and the double below it, and NaN | [`math::tests::truthiness_threshold_is_half_inclusive`](../crates/gm/src/math.rs), [`value::tests::truthiness_and_logic`](../crates/gm/src/value.rs) |
| Real comparisons use a tolerance of **1e-13**: `0.1 + 0.2 == 0.3`. They subtract, and `!=`, `>` and `>=` are the negations of `==`, `<=` and `<`, so when `a - b` is a NaN (a NaN operand, or `inf == inf`) `==`, `<` and `<=` are false and `!=`, `>` and `>=` true. `if (nan)` is false. | runner; 5,429 comparisons | [GM8](../tools/gm8-probe/README.md) (9e-14 equal, 1.1e-13 not; NaN from either side: probes `nonfinite`, `ops`); the oracle matches (patch 0014) | [`math::tests::comparisons_use_epsilon`](../crates/gm/src/math.rs), [`a_nan_difference_makes_ne_gt_and_ge_true`](../crates/gm/src/math.rs) |
| Comparing a real with a string is an error, not false; strings compare bytewise (`"B" < "a"`). | runner | OpenGMK (`gml/value.rs` `gml_cmp_impl!`) | [`value::tests::comparisons`](../crates/gm/src/value.rs) |
| `switch` matches with exact equality (not the 1e-13 tolerance; a real never matches a string) and falls through. | runner; 107 switches | OpenGMK (`gml/runtime.rs` `Instruction::Switch`, `gml/value.rs` `almost_equals`) | `crates/gm/src/ops.rs` `switch_matching_is_exact` |
| A script with no `return` on some path returns the **last assigned or called value**, not 0. `scrGetKey`, `scrGetJoy` and `scrGetChar` are used as values (47 call sites). | runner | OpenGMK (`gml/runtime.rs` `return_value`) | [`scripts_return_their_last_assignment`](../crates/transpile/tests/emit.rs) (translation) |
| `obj.v op= x` reads `v` from the **first** instance of `obj` and writes the result to **all** of them, evaluating the base twice. Plain `obj.v = x` writes every instance. | runner; 15 sites, e.g. `oMoonRoom.baskets += 1` | OpenGMK (`gml/compiler.rs` `make_modify_instruction`) | `crates/gm/tests/vars.rs` `target_access_reads_the_first_and_writes_all` |
| `op=` ignores a type error and keeps the old value, on **every** name: user, global and local variables, built-in variables (instance or game-wide, arrays included, through any base: GM8 was probed bare, through `self.` and through an object name) and the arguments (`argumentN`, `argument[n]`). So `s -= 1` on a string does nothing, and neither does `x += "a"` or `argument0 /= 2` on a string argument. OpenGMK raises the error on built-ins and the arguments (§7). Port: `gm` `ops.rs` `lenient`. | runner | GM8 (`opassign` probe in `tools/gm8-probe/results/`: all 7 operators, both operand orders, on `x`, `self.x`, `oScreen.x`, `score`, `room_caption`, `view_xview[0]`, `argument0`, `argument15`, `argument[0]` and a field) | `crates/gm/src/ops.rs` `lenient_keeps_the_old_value_on_type_errors`; [`compound_assignment_is_lenient_on_every_name`](../crates/transpile/tests/emit.rs) (translation) |
| Evaluation order: assignment target, then array index, then value; arguments and operands left to right. Matters wherever `rand` appears twice in one statement. | runner | OpenGMK (`gml/runtime.rs`) | [`values_that_call_are_bound_before_the_write`](../crates/transpile/tests/emit.rs) (translation) |
| `break` and `continue` inside `with` act on the `with` loop, not an enclosing loop. | runner; e.g. `scripts/moveTo.gml` | OpenGMK (`gml/runtime.rs` `Instruction::With`) | [`with_swaps_self_and_other_and_restores_them`](../crates/transpile/tests/emit.rs) (translation); `flow-highscores-reset-crush.txt` (`moveTo`'s, §1) |
| `real * string` repeats the string (`3 * "ab"` is `"ababab"`); `string * real` is an error. The count is an integer argument (§3: half to even, so `2.5 * "a"` is `"aa"`; through an `Int64`, cut to 32 bits, so `(2^32 + 2) * "a"` is `"aa"` and `(-2^32 + 3) * "a"` `"aaa"`; from 2^63 up `round`'s error) and runs as a `repeat` count does: none if it's below 1, but 2^31 times for -2^31, so `2^31 * "a"` stops the game with "Unexpected error occured when running the game." (GM8 fails to allocate 2 GB; `2^31 * ""` is `""`, and `(2^31 + 1) * "a"` is `""`). Far shorter strings stop it the same way: its 32-bit runner builds 304 MiB (`19 * 2^24 * "a"`) but runs out of memory at 312 MiB (`39 * 2^23 * "a"`), counting bytes, not copies (2^23 copies of a 64-byte string stop it), on a real Intel CPU and under Rosetta alike; the port and the oracle stop past 304 MiB (§7, 851-3282). | runner (unused by the game) | [GM8](../tools/gm8-probe/README.md) on a real Intel CPU and under Rosetta (probes `mulstr`, `mulstr-2p31`, `mulstr-2p31+1`, `mulstr-2p31-empty`, `mulstr-2p32`, `mulstr-2p63`, and `mulstr-2p27` to `mulstr-2p30`, `mulstr-19x2p24`, `mulstr-39x2p23`, `mulstr-2p22-64`, ... for the lengths: the error from 2^63 up is Intel's, where Rosetta gives `""`; 851-3272). OpenGMK (`gml/value.rs` `Value::mul`) saturates the count to an `i64` before cutting it to 32 bits and repeats a negative one none, so `2^31 * "a"` is `""`; the oracle follows GM8 (patch 0014) | [`value::tests::real_times_string_repeats`](../crates/gm/src/value.rs), [`real_times_string_stops_where_gm8_runs_out_of_memory`](../crates/gm/src/value.rs), [`gm8_probe.rs`](../crates/gm/tests/gm8_probe.rs) |
| Built-in functions never fail on types: a string argument reads as 0 where a real is expected, a real as `""` where a string is. | runner | OpenGMK (`gml/value.rs` `From<Value>`) | [`value::tests::function_argument_coercion`](../crates/gm/src/value.rs) |

## 3. Numbers and strings

| Quirk | Where | Status | Test |
|---|---|---|---|
| `round` is round-half-to-even (`round(2.5) == 2`) into a 64-bit integer: a negative value that rounds to 0 gives +0. **Past the `Int64` range it's an error**: GM8 runs with the x87's invalid-operation exception unmasked, so the `FISTP` behind `round`, `floor`, `ceil`, `div`, `mod` and the bitwise operators traps from 2^63 up (and on infinities and NaN), and the game stops with "Unexpected error occured when running the game." (GM8's spelling); once it has started, the trap only drops the rest of the frame (§5). | runner; 78 `round` calls | [GM8](../tools/gm8-probe/README.md) (probes `numbers`, `ops`, `in-range`), ties, -0.5 and past 2^31 included. | **Dividing by zero is an error**: `x / 0` and `x div 0` stop the game with "Division by 0.", `x mod 0` with "Modulo by 0.", for -0 too; any other divisor divides (`1 / 1e-300` is 1e300; an overflow is the next row's error). (at its start; after that they drop the rest of the frame, §5), and so does a comparison whose difference overflows (`2^1023 > -2^1023`: comparisons subtract). | **Real literals aren't correctly rounded.** The compiler reads a literal a character at a time, in doubles, every operation rounding: before the point `v = (v * 10 + c) - 48`, with `c` the digit's character code, and after it `f = f / 10` then `v = d * f + v`. So `0.6` is `0.6000000000000001`, `0.3` `0.30000000000000004`, `0.15` `0.15000000000000002` and even `0.375` `0.37500000000000006`, while `0.5`, `0.25`, `1.5` and `2.4` come out exact or correctly rounded. Whole numbers are exact below 2^53 - 48; from there `v * 10 + c` passes 2^53 and rounds before the 48 comes off, so `9007199254740989` (2^53 - 3) is 2^53 - 4 and `9007199254740991` is 2^53 (likewise at the top of every higher binade: `9223372036854774784`, 2^63 - 1024, is 2^63 - 2048). Hex literals go the same way, `v * 16` plus the code (and 10 for a letter) less that of `0`, `A` or `a`, so from 2^53 - 97 up they round too: `$1FFFFFFFFFFFFE` (2^53 - 2) is 2^53 - 1, and `$FFFFFFFFFFFFFFFF` 2^64 - 2048. Every fractional literal carries its last bit into the game's arithmetic (`image_speed = 0.6`, `yVel * 0.3`, `oYellowBall`'s `yVel += 0.15`, ...). OpenGMK reads all the digits as one whole number times 0.1 per decimal, 1 ulp off GM8 on some literals: of the game's 24 distinct fractional literals only `0.15` (once, in `oYellowBall`); oracle patch 0013 gives it GM8's reading. | compiler; every number literal in the source | The runner's literal-reading routine (its machine code: x87 `fmul`/`fadd`/`fsub` of 10, the character code and 48, each stored as a double), and all 195 literals of the [`literals` probe](../tools/gm8-probe/README.md) on GM8: the game's 24 fractional ones, decimals on which candidate readings differ, integers from 2^31 to 10^30 and hex. The x87 rounds to 53 bits while it compiles: the same 195 bits under Rosetta and on a real Intel CPU, subnormal literals included (`results/gm8-wine/literals.txt`, `results/gm8-intel/literals.txt`) | [`gm8_literals.rs`](../crates/gml/tests/gm8_literals.rs) (port and oracle against GM8, bit for bit), [`gml` `lexer::tests::numbers`](../crates/gml/src/lexer.rs), [`reals_print_as_their_gm8_source`](../crates/gml/tests/parser.rs) |
| `make_color_rgb(r, g, b)` doesn't clamp: it's `r + g * 256 + b * 65536` on its components rounded into `Int64`s (**not** cut to 32 bits as other integer arguments are), in 64 bits that wrap, so out-of-range components carry into the next channel and adding is not ORing: `(256, 0, 0)` is `(0, 1, 0)`, `(-1, 1, 0)` is 255, `(-1, 0, 1)` 65535, `(-256, 1, 0)` is 0, `(2^32 + 1, 0, 0)` is 4294967297, `(0, 0, 2^47)` is -2^63 and `(0, 0, 2^48)` is 0. A component from 2^63 up is `round`'s error. | runner; 4 calls: `characterDrawEvent`'s red reaches 260, which carries into green the same either way | [GM8](../tools/gm8-probe/README.md) on a real Intel CPU and under Rosetta (probes `color-components`, `int-args`, `color-2p63`; 851-3270; `color-components`' last two lines, red and blue overlapping, under Rosetta only, whose integer arithmetic is the hardware's). OpenGMK ORs 32-bit components (`gml/kernel.rs`); the oracle follows GM8 (patch 0014) | [`math::tests::make_color_rgb_adds_64_bit_components`](../crates/gm/src/math.rs), [`gm8_probe.rs`](../crates/gm/tests/gm8_probe.rs) |
| `string(x)` of a real equal to its own `round` (above: an `Int64`) prints that integer, so -0 is `"0"` and 1 + 1e-14 isn't whole; where that `round` traps (from 2^63 up, infinities, NaN), `string` catches it and gives `"ERROR"`. Anything else is Delphi's `FloatToStrF(x, ffFixed, 10, 2)` with the x87 at 53 bits: 18 digits from one scaling by a power of ten (rounded to a double), rounded half up at two decimals or 10 significant digits, whichever comes first. So `string(0.125) == "0.13"`, `string(1.005) == "1.00"` (just below the tie), a negative that rounds to zero has no sign (`"0.00"` for -0.004), `string(123456789.125) == "123456789.10"`, and past 10 integer digits it's `"1.099511628E12"`, `"1E15"`. | runner; 185 calls, the HUD and save files | [GM8](../tools/gm8-probe/README.md) (probes `numbers`, `strings`: 65 values chosen to tell rounding rules apart; `"ERROR"` is GM8's on a real Intel CPU, `string(sin(1e22))`, where Rosetta prints `"1E22"`); the oracle matches (patch 0014) | [`string::tests::real_to_string_uses_zero_or_two_decimals`](../crates/gm/src/string.rs); [`gm8_probe.rs`](../crates/gm/tests/gm8_probe.rs) checks every real any probe printed |
| `string_format(x, total, decimals)` is Delphi's `FloatToStrF(x, ffFixed, 18, decimals)` (as `string`, but 18 significant digits and at most 18 decimals), right-aligned in `total` characters: `string_format(0.125, 10, 2) == "      0.13"`, `string_format(1/3, 0, 18) == "0.333333333333333312"`, `string_format(1e20, 0, 2) == "1E20"`, and `"INF"`, `"-INF"`, `"NAN"` (under Rosetta: §7). Big values keep 18 digits: `string_format(2^63, 0, 2) == "9.22337203685477632E18"`, `string_format(2^1023, 0, 2) == "8.98846567431158016E307"`. A negative `decimals` rounds half up to a multiple of `10^-decimals` and prints 18 decimals (`string_format(125, 0, -1) == "130.000000000000000000"`). Strings pass through unpadded. | runner; not used by the game | [GM8](../tools/gm8-probe/README.md) (probes `string-format`, `string-format-neg`, `string-format-big`), the same under Rosetta and on a real Intel CPU (851-3259); the oracle matches (patch 0014) | [`string::tests::string_format_pads_and_rounds`](../crates/gm/src/string.rs), [`gm8_probe.rs`](../crates/gm/tests/gm8_probe.rs) |

## 4. Randomness

| Quirk | Where | Status | Test |
|---|---|---|---|
| The generator is Delphi's `Random`: `seed = seed * 0x08088405 + 1 (mod 2^32)`, and `random(x)` is `(seed as unsigned) / 2^32 * x`. `floor(random(n))` is exact for the game's integer ranges. | runner | OpenGMK (`gml/rand.rs`) + Oracle (per-frame seeds, `oDark` values); confirmed against [GM8](../tools/gm8-probe/README.md) (ten draws after `random_set_seed(12345)`, and seeds -1, 2^32+7 and 2.5) | [`rng::tests::oracle_rand_values_from_replay_seed`](../crates/gm/src/rng.rs), [`oracle_per_frame_seeds_are_reachable`](../crates/gm/src/rng.rs), [`random_is_in_range_and_rand_script_is_inclusive`](../crates/gm/src/rng.rs) |
| One global stream: level generation, gameplay and cosmetic particles share it, so a single extra or missing call anywhere changes every later level. The game never seeds it. | runner; [source-inventory §6](source-inventory.md#6-randomness) | OpenGMK | test TODO (needs the runtime) |
| At startup the runner randomizes, then draws `Random(100000)` (one more per name that already exists) to name its temp directory, before any game code. OpenGMK records the replay's start seed **after** those draws, so replays seed with the recorded value. | runner | OpenGMK (`game.rs` `Game::launch`, `make_temp_dir`) | [`rng::tests::startup_consumes_one_draw`](../crates/gm/src/rng.rs) |

## 5. Runner: frame, instances and events

The whole frame is in [runtime.md §1](design/runtime.md#1-the-frame). These are the rules most likely to be
"tidied up" by mistake. The **Port** column says where `crates/gm/src/` reproduces each one, and tests are in
`crates/gm/tests/` unless a file is named.

| Quirk | Where | Status | Port | Test |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Animation end runs after draw**: `image_index` advances and animation-end events fire at the very end of the frame, so an instance destroyed by its animation-end handler was already drawn on its last frame and goes away next frame. | runner; 51 animation-end events | OpenGMK (`game.rs` `Game::frame`) | `frame.rs` `World::step` | `frame.rs` `animation_end_comes_after_draw` |
| An instance whose sprite doesn't exist has 0 frames, so with a non-negative `image_speed` its animation-end event fires every frame. | runner | OpenGMK (`game.rs` `Game::frame`, animation) | `frame.rs` `animate` | test TODO |
| **`image_index` against the frame count is Delphi's 32-bit `Floor`.** The animation stage (Spelunky.exe 0x53e775) stores `image_index + image_speed`, then wraps it (and runs animation end) if `Floor` of it is at least the sprite's frame count; `sprite_index = s` (0x4ab0f0) stores the sprite, then resets `image_index` to 0 if the frame count is at most `Floor` of it; the default draw (0x4bf560) draws frame `Floor(image_index)`. `Floor` returns an `Integer`, so it wraps from 2^31: with one frame, 2^32 + 0.5 is frame 0 and doesn't wrap (2^32 + 1.5 does, to 2^32 + 0.5), 2^31 + 0.5 is -2^31 and doesn't, and with 10 frames 2^32 + 1.5 survives `sprite_index` (OpenGMK compared the doubles). On Intel each traps (§7): the sum past the largest double unstored, the `Floor` from 2^63 up after the store (the sprite and the new `image_index` stay); the default draw's even with nothing recorded. | runner; never in play (the game's `image_index` stays small) | GM8 on a real Intel CPU and under Rosetta (probes `anim-trap`, `sprite-floor`, `draw-floor-trap`, 851-3301) and the runner's code; the oracle matches (patch 0023) | `frame.rs` `animate`; `instance.rs` `set_sprite_index`; `drawing.rs` `draw_instance` | [`gm8_probe.rs`](../crates/gm/tests/gm8_probe.rs) `main_loop` |
| **The room's background layers scroll after the draw**, each storing `x + hspeed`, then `y + vspeed` (Spelunky.exe 0x4af130), every layer whether shown or not. On Intel a sum past the largest double traps unstored, before the later sums (§7). | runner; the game never scrolls one | GM8 on a real Intel CPU and under Rosetta (probe `bg-trap`, 851-3301); the oracle matches (patch 0023) | `frame.rs` `World::frame` | [`gm8_probe.rs`](../crates/gm/tests/gm8_probe.rs) `main_loop` |
| **GM8 stores each instance's bounding box**, and recomputes it (0x4aa958) in the setters of its inputs, once they've stored the new value: the position (0x4abf7c, also behind the step's move, the collision stage's moves back, `move_snap`, and `place_meeting` and `instance_place`, which move the instance there and back, 0x4e46fc), `image_xscale`, `image_yscale` and `image_angle` (each doing nothing when written the value it holds, and the position unless both coordinates are unchanged), `sprite_index` and `mask_index` (always), and the end of an instance's constructor. The routine copies the mask's box into the instance, then overwrites the left, right, top and bottom sides in turn, each Delphi's `Round` into an `Int64` cut to 32 bits (an unrotated box swaps reversed sides after each axis); a rotated box first stores its scaled offsets as doubles. So on Intel a side past the `Int64` range, or a rotated offset past the largest double, traps (§7) with the sides before it computed and the rest the mask's own, and that box stays until the next setter: a `core` with `sTunnelLeft` (origin 8, 8, box 2, 0, 13, 15) moved to x = 2^63 has the box 2, 0, 13, 15, at y = 2^63 94, 0, 105, 15. `x = x` there does nothing; `place_meeting` from there traps on the move back, with the answer lost, and to there on the move there, with the instance left there. `move_snap` (0x4e4a10) rounds each quotient into an `Int64` itself, which traps from 2^63 before anything is stored, then sets both coordinates at once. An instance created where its box traps is worse: the room's add routine counts it in its list before constructing it (0x4aedb7), so the slot stays empty or stale, and every later frame faults at its first loop over the instances, with no event run again. | runner; never in play (the game's positions and scales stay small) | GM8 on a real Intel CPU and under Rosetta (probes `bbox-trap`, `bbox-scale`, `bbox-place`, `bbox-move`, `bbox-create`, 851-3301) and the runner's code; the oracle matches (patch 0023), but for the creation (§7) | `instance.rs` `Instance::gm8_bbox`; `world.rs` `World::check_bbox`; `frame.rs` `set_position`; `collision.rs` `test_at`; `access.rs` `set_builtin` | `gm8_probe.rs` `main_loop`; `collision_props.rs` |
| **A room change stops the frame after the stage that asked for it.** While `room_goto`, `room_restart` or `game_end` is pending no event runs at all (`instance_destroy` destroys without the destroy event, for one), so the rest of that stage's events are skipped, and so are the later stages. Work in the same stage that isn't an event still finishes: an animation-end handler's `room_goto` (`oPlayer1` at a level exit) doesn't stop the other instances' `image_index` advancing. The switch happens between frames. | runner; e.g. `objects/oPlayer1/other_animation_end.gml` | OpenGMK (`game/events.rs` `run_instance_event`, `game.rs` `Game::frame`) | `frame.rs` `World::step`, `kernel.rs` `run_handler` | `frame.rs` `a_room_change_stops_the_frame_after_the_stage`, `events.rs` `no_event_runs_while_a_room_change_is_pending` |
| **An exception after the game's start drops the rest of the frame.** GM8's main loop runs each iteration (the frame, its room change, the message pump) inside a `try` with an empty `except` (Spelunky.exe 0x53e8aa), so an exception, which at game start (the first room's load, inside 0x545ee0's handler) stops it with "Unexpected error occured when running the game.", afterwards silently drops the rest of that iteration: the events still to run, of every instance, the later stages (motion, collisions, end step, draw, animation), the frame's room change, and the end of its input, so this frame's key presses are still pressed in the next (0x513ec8 clears them at the frame's end). The next frame goes on from what the exception left, nothing undone: a half-run event, an `instance_create`d instance whose create event was cut short, a room loaded up to the create event that trapped (the placed instances after it exist, their create events never run; no room start events, no frame-0 draw). `self` and `other` aren't left over. A room change requested before the exception stays pending, so the next frame runs no events (the event runners at 0x4b20b0 and 0x4b20d8 skip while one is) and then makes it; one requested in a room end event is lost (0x53e270 clears the pending change for those events and restores it after, unguarded). `game_end`'s room end and game end events run after GM8 leaves the loop (0x53eb88), where an exception stops it. The exceptions are the x87's traps (§3: integers past the `Int64` range, arithmetic past the largest double) and running out of memory; GML's own errors (type errors, division by zero, `sqrt` of a negative, and the built-ins that catch a trap themselves: `ln`, `exp`, `arcsin`, `real`, ..., "Error in function ln().", 0x55093c) show GM8's error dialog wherever they happen. | runner; nothing in the game reaches these values (§7) | GM8 on a real Intel CPU (probes `late-trap`, `late-speedwrite-2p63`, `late-scope`, `late-room-trap`, `late-create-trap`, `late-instcreate-trap`, `late-draw-trap`, `late-mulstr`, `late-div0`, `late-ln0`, 851-3290) and the runner's code (Spelunky.exe 0x53e810, its main loop); the oracle matches (patch 0022) | `frame.rs` `World::step`; `error.rs` `Error::is_exception` | `frame.rs` `an_exception_after_the_start_drops_the_rest_of_the_frame`, `exceptions_stop_the_game_while_it_starts_and_ends`; [`gm8_probe.rs`](../crates/gm/tests/gm8_probe.rs) `main_loop` |
| **Motion moves each instance in turn**: friction, gravity, then the move, for one instance before the next (Spelunky.exe 0x53e670), where OpenGMK does every instance's friction and gravity before any move. The same, unless one traps: then the instances before it have moved and those after haven't (above). Each step can trap on Intel. Friction computes `speed - friction` (`+` for a negative speed) as a double, then writes 0 if the sign flipped and the sum otherwise through the speed setter (0x4ac034). Gravity is AddMotion (0x4abfbc): `hspeed`'s sum is stored, then `vspeed`'s, each through a store that traps past the largest double (that one isn't stored, and `vspeed`'s isn't computed after `hspeed`'s traps), then the speed and direction update. The move computes `x + hspeed` and `y + vspeed` before storing either (and so does the collision stage's solid rule, 0x539d48).
