| **View following** (Spelunky.exe 0x4beca4): on each axis the target's position is rounded half to even; if twice the border is less than the view's size (a 32-bit sum, which wraps: a border from 2^30 keeps the rule, and a 101-wide view with a border of 50 too) the view moves just enough to keep the border, else it centres; then it's clamped to the room with the right/bottom edge applied last, so a view larger than the room gets a negative position; **then** the move from where the view was is limited to `view_hspeed`/`view_vspeed` px (unlimited if negative), so a view outside the room comes back at that speed, and at speed 0 never moves. A 100-wide view at 900 in a 960-wide room, following 905 with a border of 10 at speed 5, goes to 895 (OpenGMK, which tests `border < size / 2` and limits the speed before it clamps: 860). All of it 32-bit integer arithmetic that wraps. Views move only in the draw stage (and `screen_redraw`). `view_object` below 100000 is an object (first instance, children included); from 100000 an instance id: the first instance with it that isn't destroyed (deactivated ones are in another list), which matters only while a persistent instance shares its id with one its room placed, destroyed by the load: the load's draw (or a `screen_redraw` before the cleanup) follows the persistent one (§7). The rounding is Delphi's `Round` of x, then y, into an `Int64`, cut to 32 bits (Spelunky.exe 0x4beca4: 2^32 + 300 follows as 300), before the view moves: on Intel a target past the `Int64` range traps (§7), with the views before it moved and this one where it was. | runner; `oScreen` follows `oPlayer1` in 26 rooms, at speed -1 with even sizes, where GM8's rules and OpenGMK's agree | GM8 on a real Intel CPU and under Rosetta (probes `view-follow`, 851-3303, `view-follow-id`, 851-3309, and `view-trap`, 851-3301) and the runner's code; the oracle matches (patches 0023, 0025, 0026) | `room.rs` `View::follow`, `list.rs` `InstanceList::live_by_id` | `rooms.rs` `views_follow_by_gm8s_border_rule`, `views_clamp_to_the_room_before_their_speed_limit`, `views_follow_an_id_past_the_instance_a_persistent_one_replaced`; `gm8_probe.rs` `view_follow`, `view_follow_id`, `main_loop` |
| **Drawing converts positions itself**, and on Intel that traps (§7) whether or not the frame is shown (Spelunky.exe, read for this only; 851-3304). The texture draws store each corner as a single, which traps from 2^128 - 2^103: the plain one (0x4a3a38; `draw_sprite`, `draw_surface`) `x - 0.5` and `w + x - 0.5` after subtracting the origin, the scaled one (0x4a3ad8; `draw_sprite_ext`, the text's characters, `draw_surface_stretched` unless `w` or `h` is 0 or less) `x` plus offsets it first stores as doubles, `-xscale * xorig - 0.5` and `w * xscale` plus that (which trap past the largest double), rotated from 0.001 radians, the angle converted as for `direction` and its `fsin` and `fcos` the angle itself from 2^63. `draw_sprite` and `draw_sprite_ext` take Delphi's 32-bit `Floor` of `image_index` for a negative frame, before they check the sprite. `draw_text` rounds a real into an `Int64` itself, where `string()` catches that and gives `"ERROR"`, then draws the characters its font has: a sprite font's frames from its first character, GM's own 32 to 127; the others it skips, `#` and all (§7). `draw_rectangle` takes `Floor` of its larger x and y; `draw_circle` stores `x - r` to `y + r` as doubles, and its centre and rim, out to `x ± r` and `y ± r` whatever `circle_precision`, as singles. The default draw uses the plain draw untransformed (scale 1, angle 0, white, opaque), the scaled one otherwise; a transform that large traps in its setter first (the box), but after storing it, so each later frame's draw traps. A background layer (0x4a459c) rounds `w * xscale` and `h * yscale` into `Int64`s and keeps 32 bits (nothing is drawn if either is 0 or less), then `x`, and `y` only where the 32-bit column (moved by whole tiles to the view's left edge if tiled) is left of the view's right edge; a view rotated by 0.01 degrees or more spans `hview / 2` further each way. Each view's projection (0x4a2440) stores the sine and cosine of `-view_angle` as singles. Tiles can't trap: GM8 only draws those in the view, and nothing in GML scales one. | runner; never in play (the game's positions, scales and angles stay small) | GM8 on a real Intel CPU and under Rosetta (probes `draw-builtin-trap`: 37 draws, `draw-default-trap`, `draw-stage-trap`, 851-3304) and the runner's code; the oracle matches (patch 0028) | `gm8.rs` (`draw_quad`, `draw_quad_ext`, `draw_is_plain`, `text_draws`, `draw_rectangle`, `draw_circle`, `view_span`, `draw_background`); `draw_builtins.rs`; `surfaces.rs`; `drawing.rs` `draw_instance`, `draw_backgrounds`, `redraw` | `math.rs` (unit) `draws_trap_where_gm8_converts_past_its_types`; [`gm8_probe.rs`](../crates/gm/tests/gm8_probe.rs) `main_loop` |

| **`speed` is snapped** after `hspeed` or `vspeed` changes, as `direction` is: it's `Sqrt(Sqr(hspeed) + Sqr(vspeed))` as `point_distance` computes it (the squares in an x87 register, §3), then replaced by a whole number within 0.0001 of it. So `hspeed = 5.00001` gives `speed` 5 (`hspeed` keeps 5.00001), `hspeed = 3.00003; vspeed = 4.00004` gives 5, and a speed under 0.0001 is 0 (`hspeed = 0.00005`, `hspeed = 2^-600`), but 0.0002 stays. The snap is Delphi's `Round` into an `Int64`, so a write of `hspeed` or `vspeed` that makes the speed 2^63 or more is `round`'s error (§3), even with each component below 2^63 (`hspeed = 3 * 2^61; vspeed = 3 * 2^61`), and one past the largest double is `point_distance`'s overflow error: on Intel the register's wider exponent never shows in `speed` (under Rosetta, which doesn't trap, components of 2^1023 give 2^1023 * sqrt(2), not inf). Gravity recomputes `speed` the same way (the runner's AddMotion calls the same update, read from its code, 851-3280), and traps the same way (851-3290, below). `direction` from the components needs nothing new: tiny and huge components give the same directions on Intel, under Rosetta and in the port. | runner; every `hspeed`/`vspeed` write, and gravity | [GM8](../tools/gm8-probe/README.md) on a real Intel CPU and under Rosetta (probes `motion-range`, `motion-snap`, `speed-2p63`, `speed-store-overflow`, 851-3281). OpenGMK doesn't snap `speed` and squares in doubles; the oracle matches (patch 0015) | `instance.rs` `Instance::update_speed_direction`, `math::speed` | `instance.rs` `components_give_a_snapped_speed_squared_in_a_register`, [`gm8_probe.rs`](../crates/gm/tests/gm8_probe.rs) |
| **The motion setters** (read from the runner's code, `Spelunky.exe` 0x4ab23c-0x4ab310, and measured, 851-3280). Each of `hspeed`, `vspeed`, `speed` and `direction` does nothing when written the value it already holds (an x87 compare, so -0 equals 0): `speed = speed` or `direction = direction` keeps components that came from `hspeed` and `vspeed`, where recomputing them would move them an ulp (`hspeed = 0.3; vspeed = 0.7; speed = speed` leaves `hspeed` at 0.3). Otherwise `hspeed` and `vspeed` store the value and recompute `speed` and `direction`, never the other component (so `hspeed = 0; vspeed = 0.5` leaves `hspeed` 0: `angle-motion-add`'s `hspeed = (k mod 7) / 4 - 0.75` reads -2^-53 at k = 3 only because GM8 reads the literal `0.75` as 0.7500000000000001, §3); `speed` and `direction` store it and recompute `hspeed` and `vspeed`. A written direction is wrapped as `d - 360 * Floor(d / 360)` with Delphi's 32-bit `Floor`: `d` modulo 360 in range, but `direction = -360` is +0, and from 360 * 2^31 up the `Floor` wraps (`direction = 360 * 2^31 + 90` stays 773094113370, since 2^31 * 360 is 0 in 32 bits); from a quotient of 2^63 up its `Trunc` traps, which stops the game on Intel (Rosetta stores `d` as it is): the runner has stored `d` by then, and the components keep their old values (851-3290). The direction recomputed from the components is 0, 90 or 270 by the sign of `vspeed` when `hspeed` is 0 of either sign (`hspeed = -0` at rest gives 0, where `atan2(-0, -0)` is 180), and wrapped too, so a direction snapped to 360 (`hspeed = 1; vspeed = 0.00000001`) is 0. AddMotion, behind gravity and `motion_add`, always recomputes `speed` and `direction`, even when the components didn't change (`motion_add(0, 0)` can move `direction` an ulp). Friction writes through the `speed` setter, so at speed 0 (`hspeed = 0.00005` snaps the speed to 0) it leaves the components as they are. | runner; every motion write, friction, gravity | GM8 on a real Intel CPU and under Rosetta (probes `motion-setters`, `direction-2p63`, `divassign-skip`); OpenGMK skips only `hspeed` and `vspeed` writes, wraps with `rem_euclid`, has no `hspeed == 0` case and skips the update when gravity changes neither component; the oracle matches GM8 (patch 0020) | `instance.rs` `set_direction`, `set_speed`, `add_motion`; `math::wrap_direction`, `x87::gm8::direction` | `instance.rs` `motion_setters_skip_the_value_already_there` and the three tests after it, [`gm8_probe.rs`](../crates/gm/tests/gm8_probe.rs) |

| **Solid collisions**: if either instance is solid, both move back to their previous positions *before* the two collision events; afterwards both get `x += hspeed; y += vspeed` again, and if they still overlap they move back a second time. The move-backs happen even while a room change is pending (when the events don't run), and `solid` is read again after the events. | runner; 12 events involve a solid | OpenGMK (`game/events.rs` `run_collisions`) | `collision.rs` `World::run_collisions` | `collision.rs` `solids_move_back_before_the_events_and_on_again_after`, `solids_keep_the_reapplied_move_when_free` |
| In the collision stage the second instance's event runs even if the first one's destroyed it, and after a meeting that leaves the first instance inactive, it meets nothing more that frame. | runner; 123 collision events | OpenGMK (`game/events.rs` `run_collisions`) | `collision.rs` `World::run_collisions` | `collision.rs` `a_destroyed_instance_meets_nothing_more` |

| `instance_nearest` measures to instances' positions, not their boxes, and keeps the first of equally near ones (draw order for `all`, list order for an object). It compares squared distances against a start of 10^10, so an instance 100000 pixels or more away is never nearest, and it finds nothing for `self`, `other` or an instance id. | runner; 80 calls | OpenGMK (`gml/kernel.rs` `instance_nearest`) | `collision.rs` `World::instance_nearest` | `collision.rs` `instance_nearest_measures_positions_and_keeps_the_first_of_a_tie` |

| **A point and a rectangle's corners are compared unrounded with a bounding box, which covers its pixels whole**: GM8's tests (Spelunky.exe 0x4ac734, 0x4ac834) pass a coordinate for `left <= v < right + 1`, so x = 47.5 or 47.99 is inside a box ending at 47 (OpenGMK, and so the port and the oracle before 851-3306, took a point's box as `x <= 47`, and rounded a rectangle's corners first, so 31.6 reached a box starting at 32). A rectangle takes its smaller and larger corner from the unrounded reals; only a precise test, once the box passes and the instance has a mask, rounds them (half to even, into an `Int64` cut to 32 bits) and walks the rounded rectangle's pixels inside the box. So a corner at 47.6 passes the box but rounds past it, a corner of 2^32 - 10 picked as the larger is -10 there (an empty rectangle, which still meets the box without `prec`), and a non-precise test converts nothing: a corner at 2^64 traps only a precise test that meets the box (§7). The precise point test rounds the point half to even. An instance's own position is rounded for its mask but not for its box, so at a .5 position the box can reach a pixel the mask can't: `instance_place` at x = 104.5 overlaps a brick's box but not its mask. | runner; 546 `collision_point` calls, often at fractional positions (`collision_point(x + 8, y + 16, ...)`), 84 `collision_rectangle` (`oJar`'s `x - 3`). It changes play: 23 of the 126 replays' fingerprints changed (851-3306) | GM8 on a real Intel CPU and under Rosetta (probes `collision-edges`, `collision-edges-trap`) and the runner's code; the oracle matches (patch 0027). OpenGMK (`instance.rs` `update_bbox`) for the instance's position | `collision.rs` `within`, `Query::rect`, `Query::hits`, `Xform` | `collision.rs` `bounding_boxes_cover_whole_pixels_for_unrounded_points_and_corners`, `precise_tests_read_the_mask_and_round_positions`, `place_queries_test_self_at_another_position`; [`gm8_probe.rs`](../crates/gm/tests/gm8_probe.rs) `collision_edges`, `main_loop` |
| **A precise test's mask frame is Delphi's 32-bit `Floor` of `image_index`, counted back from the last frame when negative**, for every test (instances, points, rectangles, lines): each test (Spelunky.exe 0x4ad1a0, 0x4ac734, 0x4ac834, ...) takes `Floor` (0x4238e8, an `Integer`), and the sprite's routine (0x4a4d84 and its siblings) its signed remainder by the frame count, plus the count if negative. So -0.5 is the last frame, 2^32 + 1.5 frame 1 and 2^31 + 0.5 (-2^31) frame 1 of 3; OpenGMK took the point, rectangle and line tests' frame as an unsigned 64-bit floor, so frame 0 for any negative index. Once the boxes meet, and the mask sprite has frames, the test takes that `Floor` (with or without separate masks) and rounds the instances' positions, the point, and the line's clipped ends with Delphi's `Round` into an `Int64` cut to 32 bits; on Intel each traps from 2^63 (§7): an `image_index` of 2^63, or one below -2^63 (-2^63 itself is frame 0), and an instance left at x = 2^63 by its box's trap, whose own box a point still meets. A non-precise test, or one whose boxes don't meet, converts nothing. A scan (`collision_point` on an object, `place_meeting`, the collision stage) stops at the first instance whose test traps; `place_meeting` and `instance_place` leave the instance where they moved it, with no move back. | runner; never in play (the game's `image_index` and positions stay small) | GM8 on a real Intel CPU and under Rosetta (probes `collision-frame`: 36 indices through each test, `collision-frame-trap`, 851-3305) and the runner's code; the oracle matches (patch 0024) | `collision.rs` `frame_mask`, `Xform::new`, `World::find_hit`, `World::test_at` | `collision.rs` (unit) `frames_are_a_32_bit_floor_counted_back_from_the_last_when_negative`; [`gm8_probe.rs`](../crates/gm/tests/gm8_probe.rs) `collision_frames`, `main_loop`; `collision_props.rs` |
| `collision_line` compares its ends with the box as the point and rectangle tests do, `left <= v < right + 1` unrounded (without `prec`, a line at x = right + 0.5 still hits; GM8's 0x4aceec, probe `collision-edges`; OpenGMK's test, which the port keeps, adds the 1 in doubles where GM8 adds it in 32 bits, which only differs for a box ending at 2^31 - 1), clips the line to the box horizontally only, and its precise test maps each unrounded point of the line with `origin + floor(offset / scale)`, unlike every other precise test. | runner; 16 calls, all precise | OpenGMK (`game.rs` `check_collision_line`, "probably not correct") | `collision.rs` `line_hits` | `collision.rs` `lines_use_gm8s_own_rules` |
| An instance without a mask sprite has the bounding box (-100000, -100000)-(-100000, -100000): a non-precise point or rectangle test there finds it, and `distance_to_object` measures to that pixel. It never collides with other instances. | runner | OpenGMK (`instance.rs` `update_bbox`, `BBOX_DEFAULT`) | `collision.rs` `NO_MASK_BBOX` | `collision.rs` `distances_are_between_bounding_boxes`, `place_queries_test_self_at_another_position` |
| `font_add_sprite` ids continue after the game file's font slots, **deleted ones included**: Spelunky's file has one deleted font, so `global.myFont` is 1 and `global.myFontSmall` 2, not 0 and 1. `first` is clamped to 0..255. | runner; `scrInit` | OpenGMK (`gml/kernel.rs` `font_add_sprite`: `fonts.len()`) + Oracle (`--fields` dump, frame 0) | `draw_builtins.rs` `font_add_sprite`; the host calls `World::set_font_slots` | `drawing.rs` `fonts_get_the_ids_after_the_game_files_and_map_glyphs_from_space` |

A negative `subimg` draws the calling instance's `floor(image_index)`. | runner | OpenGMK (`gml/kernel.rs`, `NonexistentAsset`) | `draw_builtins.rs` | `drawing.rs` `headless_draws_record_nothing_but_still_check_sprites`, `each_draw_records_one_command_with_the_settings_baked_in` |
| **Text files** (`file_text_*`): `file_text_read_string` stops *before* the line end (the `\r\n`, or the `\n` of a bare-LF line; a lone `\r` is part of the line), so reading twice without `file_text_readln` gives `""`; `readln` skips past the next `\n`; `file_text_eof` is only true with no bytes left, so after reading a line that ends in a newline it's false until `readln`. At the end, reads give `""`. `file_text_writeln` writes `\r\n`. `file_text_open_read` of a missing file returns -1 (false to `if (file)`). ## 6. Renderer (GM8 on Direct3D 8)

From the [`draw` crate docs](../crates/draw/src/lib.rs). Pixel output isn't game state, but the oracle comparison
of screenshots depends on these.

| Quirk | Where | Status | Test |
|---|---|---|---|
| A view's **port is ignored while a surface is the target** (D3D8's viewport call does nothing then), so the view is stretched over the whole surface. `oScreen`'s view-scaling trick relies on it. | runner; `oScreen` | OpenGMK (`render/opengl.rs`, "DX8's viewport function doesn't do anything if a surface is set") | [`surfaces_ignore_the_port_and_frames_keep_their_start_state`](../crates/app/src/render.rs) |
| **Alpha is blended with the colour factors** (D3D8 had no separate alpha blend), so drawing on a surface with `bm_normal` leaves translucent alpha, which the game's "alpha fix" rectangle (`bm_src_color, bm_one` in black) repairs. | runner; `oScreen` | OpenGMK (`render/opengl.rs` uses `glBlendFunc`, not the separate form) | [`dark_level_composition_lights_only_the_circles`](../crates/app/src/render.rs) |
| `draw_rectangle`'s **far corner is inclusive**: `draw_rectangle(0, 0, 10, 10)` fills 11x11 pixels. Sprites, backgrounds, tiles and surfaces get GM's half-pixel shift instead; rectangles, lines and circles don't. | runner | OpenGMK (`render/opengl.rs` `draw_rectangle`) | test TODO |
| **Depth ties**: instances and tiles each keep a persistent list that is *stably* re-sorted by depth (highest first) at every draw, so equal depths keep their previous order (creation order unless a depth changed). At equal depth an instance draws before a tile. | runner | OpenGMK (`InstanceList::draw_sort`, `Game::draw_view`) + Oracle (rTutorial screenshot, 851-3130) | [`highest_depth_first_with_creation_order_ties`](../crates/app/src/preview.rs), [`instances_beat_tiles_on_equal_depth`](../crates/app/src/preview.rs) |
| **Drawing state persists across steps**: a blend mode set by `draw_set_blend_mode` stays until changed, and `oScreen` leaves its surface as the target at the end of every step for the next draw stage and begin step. | runner; `oScreen` | OpenGMK (nothing resets it per frame) | [`draw_state_follows_targets_views_and_blends`](../crates/draw/src/lib.rs), [`blend_modes_persist_across_targets_and_steps`](../crates/gm/tests/surfaces.rs), [`surfaces_ignore_the_port_and_frames_keep_their_start_state`](../crates/app/src/render.rs) |
| **Surface ids** are the lowest free slot, from 0, and a freed id is reused, so `oScreen`'s `pSurf`, `screen` and `darkSurf` are 0, 1 and 2 (and `if (screen)` holds). `surface_create` never fails on a working machine, so `oScreen`'s `screen == -1` fallback never runs. A side outside 1 to `draw::MAX_SURFACE_SIZE` (2048) is an error, as in the oracle, where OpenGL fails and OpenGMK raises a GML error; GM8 returned -1 at a machine-dependent limit. The game only asks for 320x240. | runner; `oScreen` | OpenGMK (`gml/kernel.rs` `surface_create`, `handleman.rs` `HandleList::put`) | [`surface_ids_are_the_lowest_free_slot`, `sizes_outside_the_limit_are_errors`](../crates/gm/src/surfaces.rs) |
| **Targets**: `surface_set_target` on an id that isn't a surface does nothing (the target stays); setting a surface or `surface_reset_target` resets the projection to the whole target (the back buffer for a reset, not the current view's port: views only apply again at the next draw stage). `surface_free` on the current target resets the target to the screen first. | runner; `oScreen` | OpenGMK (`gml/kernel.rs` `surface_set_target`, `surface_reset_target`, `surface_free`; `render/opengl.rs` `set_target`, `reset_target`) | [`targets_switch_and_reset_the_projection`](../crates/gm/src/surfaces.rs) |
| `draw_surface` and `draw_surface_stretched` draw white and opaque whatever `draw_set_color`/`draw_set_alpha` say. **A surface that doesn't exist is an error**, "Trying to use non-existing surface.", checked before anything else (Spelunky.exe 0x556a80), and **`draw_surface_stretched` with a width or height of 0 or less draws nothing** (its draw, 0x4a10c0, tests both against 0). OpenGMK drew nothing for the first and the surface mirrored for the second; **fixed in the oracle (patch 0031) and the port (851-3308)**. `draw_surface_ext` and `draw_surface_part` make the same check, from the runner's code (not probed): the game calls neither, the port has neither, and the oracle's still draw nothing. The game only draws its own surfaces, which it re-creates when `surface_exists` fails, at positive sizes. `draw_clear` is opaque and keeps the colour's low 24 bits. | runner | GM8 on a real Intel CPU and under Rosetta (probes `draw-skips`, `surface-missing`, `surface-missing-stretched`); OpenGMK (`gml/kernel.rs` `draw_surface_ext`, `draw_clear`) | [`surface_draws_ignore_the_drawing_colour`, `stretches_draw_where_gm8_did`, `drawing_a_missing_surface_is_an_error`](../crates/gm/src/surfaces.rs) |
| **`screen_redraw` is the draw stage** (view following, depth sort, every draw event) into the current target, then a present; `screen_refresh` only presents. A tick that calls either counts as shown even if it then fails or ends with a room change pending, and the window shows the back buffer as it was at the last present (`draw::Command::Present`), whatever a later room change does to it. `oScreen` pauses by `screen_redraw` into `pSurf`, so that step runs every draw event twice. | runner; `oScreen` | OpenGMK (`gml/kernel.rs` `screen_redraw`, `screen_refresh`) | [`screen_redraw_runs_each_draw_event_once_more_into_the_target`](../crates/gm/tests/surfaces.rs), [`screen_refresh_shows_a_tick_that_ends_with_a_room_change_or_fails`](../crates/gm/tests/surfaces.rs) |
| `action_fullscreen` (1 windowed, 2 fullscreen, else switch) changes nothing GML reads; OpenGMK ignores it. The port keeps the request for the frontend. | runner; `oScreen` create | OpenGMK (`gml/kernel.rs` `action_fullscreen`) | [`fullscreen_follows_action_fullscreen`](../crates/gm/tests/surfaces.rs) |
| Unknown `bm_*` factors are `bm_zero`; `draw_set_blend_mode` with an unknown mode is `bm_normal`. | runner | OpenGMK (`gml/kernel.rs`) | [`gml_blend_values`](../crates/draw/src/lib.rs) |
| Sprite fonts are monospaced: every character advances by the cell width plus the separation. **A byte the font lacks draws nothing, but the cursor still advances**: GM8's character draw (Spelunky.exe 0x5193a8) skips a byte outside the font's range, and its line advances by the byte's width (0x518ee4), the space's for a missing one, or the first character's if the font has no space (in a monospaced font, the cell). OpenGMK drew the font's space; **fixed in the oracle (patch 0031) and the renderer (851-3308)**. `#` starts a line, `\#` is a literal `#`. | runner; `font_add_sprite` fonts | GM8 on a real Intel CPU and under Rosetta (probe `draw-skips`: a font whose space draws `'!'`'s frame leaves the gap empty); OpenGMK (`create_chars_from_sprite`, `draw_string`) | [`draw` text tests](../crates/draw/src/lib.rs) (`missing_characters_draw_nothing_but_advance`, `missing_space_only_advances`, `hash_breaks_lines_unless_escaped`) |
| `draw_sprite` draws white and opaque whatever `draw_set_color`/`draw_set_alpha` say; `draw_text`, `draw_rectangle` and `draw_circle` use them. `draw_text` shows a real as `string()` does (`2.50`), but from 2^63 traps where `string()` gives `"ERROR"` (§5). A character the font lacks draws nothing, an escaped `\#` included (above). | runner | OpenGMK (`gml/kernel.rs` `draw_sprite`, `draw_text`; `game/draw.rs` `LineIterator`) | `drawing.rs` `each_draw_records_one_command_with_the_settings_baked_in`; [`an_escaped_hash_the_font_lacks_draws_nothing`](../crates/draw/src/lib.rs) |

## 7. Where the oracle isn't GM8

The oracle (OpenGMK in Docker, [tools/oracle/README.md](../tools/oracle/README.md#caveats)) is our best evidence,
not proof. These are known differences from 1.1 on Windows; trace diffs must allow for them.

| Caveat | Effect | Status | Test |
|---|---|---|---|
| OpenGMK is a reimplementation, and models Delphi's `Double` as plain IEEE `f64` arithmetic. The real runner runs on the x87 FPU. **Checked (851-3168):** GM8 runs GML with the x87 rounding to 53 bits (a sum and a product that round differently through 64 bits, or 24 as Direct3D's default mode would, come out as plain doubles), so `+ - * /` and `sqrt` give IEEE double results, as on OpenGMK. | None for arithmetic: the `precision` probe, 1000 sums of `0.1`, 100 products of `1.1`, the harmonic sum and subnormal results all match bit for bit. | Confirmed against [GM8](../tools/gm8-probe/README.md) (under Rosetta, which honors the precision control: `tools/gm8-probe/x87`) | `crates/gm/tests/gm8_probe.rs` |
| **Transcendentals and GM8's 80-bit math.** GM8 does its math on the x87 FPU: arithmetic rounds to 53 bits, but Delphi's constants (`Pi`, `Pi / 180`) and the transcendental instructions' results stay 80-bit in registers, and `fsin`, `fcos` and `fptan` reduce by Intel's 66-bit pi. **Measured on a real Intel CPU (851-3247)**, ~23,000 inputs (`probes/x87/`): against correctly rounded doubles, `degtorad` is 1 ulp off on 11% of half degrees, `point_direction` on 22% of grid points, the runner's `direction` on 18% and `hspeed`/`vspeed` from `direction` on 35% (1-3 ulps, up to hundreds near zero); `sin`, `cos` and `tan` are off at multiples of pi/2 (`sin(180°)` is 1.2246063538223773e-16) and on 13-32% of arguments up to 30,000 radians, and `sin(1e22)` is `1e22` (fsin's out-of-range case). Unpatched, OpenGMK would use its platform's libm instead (851-3167, 851-3173). **Decision (851-3257):** the port emulates the x87 (`gm::math::x87`, the vendored pxfm's `x87` module: an 80-bit register type with the x87's rounding, and the transcendentals computed exactly at 128 bits and rounded to 64), and the oracle's patch 0015 routes OpenGMK's `sin`, `cos`, `tan`, `arctan`, `arctan2`, `degtorad`, `radtodeg`, `point_direction`, `lengthdir_x`/`lengthdir_y` and motion (`update_hvspeed`, `update_speed_direction`) through the same code. The formulas: `degtorad(x)` is `x * (Pi / 180)` and `radtodeg(x)` `x * (180 / Pi)` with 80-bit constants (Delphi's `DegToRad`, `RadToDeg`); `point_direction` and `direction` are `fpatan(-dy, dx) * 180 / Pi`, each step rounded to 53 bits, `+ 360` if negative (so straight right is -0, and equal points give +0); `hspeed`/`vspeed` are `speed * fcos(direction * Pi / 180)` and `-speed * fsin(...)` with the angle rounded per step, then snapped to a whole number within 0.0001, and a snapped zero is +0 (Delphi's `Round` gives an integer; OpenGMK kept -0 for `vspeed` at direction 0). `lengthdir_x`/`lengthdir_y` are exactly `hspeed`/`vspeed`, snap included (measured under Rosetta, 851-3257: OpenGMK doesn't snap them). `sqrt` and `hypot` were already right, and so was `point_distance` but for its x87 register's wider exponent, which the port and patch 0015 model too since 851-3271 (an `fsqrt` in `x87`: §3); the runner's `speed` from `hspeed`/`vspeed` is that `point_distance`, snapped (§5, 851-3281). GM8's `exp`, `ln` and `power` (not GML built-ins in the port: the game never calls them) are modelled too, in `x87::gm8` (Delphi's `Exp`: `x * log2(e)` rounded to 53 bits, `f2xm1`, `+ 1`, `fscale`; `Power(x, y)` as `Exp(y * Ln(x))` for fractional `y`), but the oracle's are left as OpenGMK's. **Angles (851-3262):** the runner converts `image_angle` (bounding boxes and the five mask tests), `gravity_direction` and `view_angle` as it does `direction` (`angle * Pi / 180`, each step rounded to 53 bits), not as `degtorad` or `f64::to_radians` do, read from its code and measured on Intel (`probes/x87/angle-*.gml`, 5,626 lines: GM8's formulas with the runner's conversion match every one; with `to_radians` they're off on 597 of the 1,422 box lines, 774 of 1,986 mask lines, 298 of 1,440 `motion_add` lines and 10 of 778 view lines, and with `degtorad`'s conversion on 579, 732, 283 and 0). Boxes and masks store `fsin`/`fcos` as doubles (`x87::gm8::sin_cos_degrees`); gravity goes through the runner's `motion_add` routine (the step calls it with `gravity_direction` and `gravity`, read from its code; no probe steps a frame, so gravity is measured through `motion_add`), `hspeed = cos(dir) * gravity + hspeed`, `vspeed = vspeed - sin(dir) * gravity` with the 80-bit results in the products and no snap (`x87::gm8::add_motion`). The port uses both, and patch 0017 routes OpenGMK's `update_bbox`, collision tests, gravity, `motion_add` and view transform through them (patch 0019 replaces the view transform with GM8's whole routine, below). | None between port and oracle, and none known from GM8: every measured line matches bit for bit, signed zeros included, `exp` and `power` too. The model of an instruction's exact result rounds wrongly only where that result lies within ~2^-120 of a halfway point, about once in 2^55 arguments; the x87's own `fsin` isn't always correctly rounded at 64 bits either, which no measured line shows. Before 851-3257, port and oracle were correctly rounded and so drifted from real 1.1 wherever the game aims or moves at an angle. | GM8 on Intel (`tools/gm8-probe/results/gm8-intel/x87/`, [README](../tools/gm8-probe/README.md#real-x87)), and under Rosetta for `radtodeg` and `lengthdir` (`results/gm8-wine/x87/extra.txt`); each x87 instruction checked against mpmath on ~200,000 arguments (`tools/gm8-probe/x87/vectors.py`) | [`math/intel.rs`](../crates/gm/src/math/intel.rs) (every Intel line), [`math::tests::transcendentals_are_gm8s`](../crates/gm/src/math.rs), the vendored pxfm's `x87` tests |
| **Bounding boxes and rotated views: GM8's own formulas.** The angle converts as GM8's runner converts it (above), but OpenGMK's formulas around it weren't GM8's. GM8's bounding box (its runner's routine at 0x4aa958) takes the mask's edges from the origin before the scale, `round((left - ox) * xscale + x)` and `round(((right - ox + 1) * xscale + x) - 1)` (swapped if reversed), where OpenGMK scales from `(x - ox * xscale) + left * xscale`; rotated, it bounds each side's products separately (`x0 = (left - ox) * xscale`, `x1 = (right - ox + 1) * xscale - 1`, `left = round((min(c*x0, c*x1) + x) + min(s*y0, s*y1))`, `top = round((min(c*y0, c*y1) + y) - max(s*x0, s*x1))`, and the maxima for the others), where OpenGMK rotates the four corners. The same box in exact arithmetic, but a side near a rounding boundary rounds the other way, unrotated boxes at fractional positions included. **Fixed in the oracle and the port (851-3278):** `Instance::bbox_at` is GM8's formula, and patch 0018 makes OpenGMK's `update_bbox` the same. GM8's mouse transform (around 0x49ba4b) doesn't rotate below 0.01 degrees (an 80-bit constant), puts the 80-bit sine and cosine of `-view_angle` straight into its products and adds in another order, `x = (c*dx' + (wview/2 + xview)) + s*dy'` and `y = c*dy' + ((hview/2 + yview) - s*dx')` with `dx'`, `dy'` the point's offset from the view's centre, where OpenGMK always rotates, with the sine and cosine as doubles, about the centre. **Fixed in the oracle and the port (851-3279):** `View::to_room` and patch 0019's `transform_point` both call `x87::gm8::view_to_room`. | None for the box between port and oracle, and none known from GM8: the port matches GM8 on Intel on all 1,422 `angle-bbox` lines (119 were off before) and the patched oracle on every one. The replays' fingerprints didn't change: a one-off instrumented run over the committed replays (851-3278, not kept) found the two formulas disagreeing on very few boxes, each an `oFlareSpark` just above the room, which no collision reaches (at `y = -3.5000000000000004` its bottom is -3 on GM8 and was -2, because OpenGMK's `y - 2` rounds to -5.5). None for views, between port and oracle or from GM8: the port matches GM8 on Intel on all 778 `angle-view` lines (11 were off before, 9 of them below 0.01 degrees); Spelunky never rotates one, and unrotated the two transforms are the same. | [GM8](../tools/gm8-probe/README.md#angles) on Intel; the runner's code (`Spelunky.exe`) | [`gm8_probe.rs`](../crates/gm/tests/gm8_probe.rs) (`rotated_bounding_boxes_convert_angles_as_gm8`, through the port's real code; `rotated_views_convert_angles_as_gm8`, through `View::to_room`) |
| **View following: GM8's border test and speed limit.** GM8 (Spelunky.exe 0x4beca4) keeps the border while `2 * border < size`, a wrapping 32-bit sum, and limits the view's speed after clamping it to the room, from where it was; OpenGMK (`game/draw.rs`) tested `border < size / 2` and limited the speed first (§5). They differ for an odd size (101 with a border of 50), a border from 2^30, a speed of 0 or more with the view outside the room or wider than it, or a clamp that undoes more than the speed (probe `view-follow`: 7 views, 28 lines). **Fixed in the oracle and the port (851-3303):** `View::follow` and patch 0025 do what GM8 does. | None in play and no fingerprint changed: `oScreen` follows at speed -1 (unlimited) with even view sizes (320x240) and borders under half of them, where the two agree. | [GM8](../tools/gm8-probe/README.md) on a real Intel CPU and under Rosetta (`view-follow`, line for line the same); the runner's code | `rooms.rs` `views_clamp_to_the_room_before_their_speed_limit`, [`gm8_probe.rs`](../crates/gm/tests/gm8_probe.rs) `view_follow` |
| **View following by instance id: GM8 skips destroyed instances.** GM8 (Spelunky.exe 0x4beca4) scans the room's array of active instances (deactivated ones are moved to another as they're deactivated) for the first with the id that isn't marked destroyed; OpenGMK (`get_by_instid`) takes the first instance with the id and follows it only if it's active. Two instances share an id only when a persistent instance arrives in a room that placed one with its id, which the load marks destroyed until the frame's cleanup; the load ends with a draw, so there (and in a `screen_redraw` before the cleanup) GM8 follows the persistent instance and OpenGMK didn't move the view (probe `view-follow-id`: `oGlobals`, persistent and placed in `rIntro`, after a `room_restart()`). A destroyed or deactivated target alone isn't followed by either. **Fixed in the oracle and the port (851-3309):** `InstanceList::live_by_id` and patch 0026 skip destroyed instances. GML's other id lookups: the next row (851-3310). | None in play and no fingerprint changed: Spelunky's views follow objects, never ids. | [GM8](../tools/gm8-probe/README.md) on a real Intel CPU and under Rosetta (`view-follow-id`, line for line the same); the runner's code | `rooms.rs` `views_follow_an_id_past_the_instance_a_persistent_one_replaced`, [`gm8_probe.rs`](../crates/gm/tests/gm8_probe.rs) `view_follow_id` |
| **Lookups by instance id: GM8 treats a shared id in three ways.** While a persistent instance shares its id with the placed one the room load destroyed (the row above, §5), OpenGMK (`get_by_instid`) let the first instance with the id decide every lookup, and so found nothing. GM8's lookups find (Spelunky.exe; probe `id-lookups`, in a create event during `rIntro`'s reload and in the next begin step): **the persistent one** for `instance_exists`, `instance_number`, `instance_find(id, 0)`, the collision tests (`collision_point`, `_rectangle`, `_line`, `instance_position`, `position_meeting`, `place_meeting`, `instance_place`; the scans from 0x4e3898) and `instance_deactivate_object`, which take the first with the id that isn't destroyed, as view following does; also for variable reads and writes through the id (`id.x`; 0x525e74, 0x526084), which scan the instance array from its end for the last with the id. They find **nothing** for `with (id)` (0x528596) and `distance_to_object(id)` (0x55b6c6, 1000000), which stop at the first with the id. **Fixed in the oracle and the port (851-3310):** `InstanceList::live_by_id`, `last_by_id` and `by_id`; patch 0030. **Not modeled:** (1) before scanning, variable access tries two array positions cached by earlier scans (shared by reads and writes; a read takes one only if not destroyed), so a write can take the destroyed instance and write nothing (`c_cached_write`: 310 on GM8, 7 on the oracle). The port keeps GM8's arrays (851-3311), so the cache could be modeled, but only the pair can tell. (2) `instance_deactivate_object` deactivates the destroyed one too, which takes it out of `instance_count` (`c_deact_count`: 431 on GM8, 432 on the oracle before patch 0032); OpenGMK never deactivated a destroyed instance. **Fixed in the oracle and the port (851-3311):** the next row. | None in play and no fingerprint changed: the pair never forms in either program (§5). | [GM8](../tools/gm8-probe/README.md) on a real Intel CPU and under Rosetta (`id-lookups`, line for line the same); the runner's code; the patched oracle matches but for (1) | `rooms.rs` `id_lookups_while_a_persistent_instance_shares_its_id`, [`gm8_probe.rs`](../crates/gm/tests/gm8_probe.rs) `id_lookups` |
| **(De)activation: GM8 moves instances between two arrays.** GM8 keeps a room's instances in an array of active instances (destroyed ones too, until the cleanup) and one of deactivated instances. Its (de)activation built-ins (Spelunky.exe 0x55c9e4 to 0x55cf78) only set or clear each instance's flag (+0x109), the deactivating ones on destroyed instances too; then 0x4af00c moves the unflagged instances of the inactive array to the **end** of the active array, then the flagged ones of the active array to the end of the inactive array, each in their order, and only if any moved rebuilds the per-object lists from the active array (0x4ad67c, 0x4ad814), destroyed instances included. The depth sort before a draw (0x4aef24, an insertion sort) reorders the active array alone, not the lists, and the cleanup (0x4aefb0) only reaches the active array. OpenGMK flipped a state in place in one list. So, on GM8 and not on OpenGMK (probe `deactivation-order`): a reactivated instance comes after the others in `with`, `instance_find`, `with (all)`, the events and, at its depth, the draw; instances reactivated together come back in the order they were deactivated, not by depth; `instance_activate_all()` (and any call) that moves nothing doesn't rebuild the lists, where OpenGMK rebuilt them on every `self`, `other` and `all`; `notme` leaves the caller in place; and a destroyed instance that is deactivated leaves `instance_count` and outlasts the cleanups until it's reactivated, then goes at the next. **Fixed in the oracle and the port (851-3311):** `InstanceList::settle`, patch 0032. | **In play, every level step:** `oLevel` deactivates everything more than 96 px outside the view and reactivates `oCharacter`, the ropes, the controllers and more, which so move to the end, changing which instance a loop, an event or a collision test reaches first: 61 of the 126 fingerprints changed. | [GM8](../tools/gm8-probe/README.md) on a real Intel CPU and under Rosetta (`deactivation-order`, line for line the same); the runner's code; the patched oracle matches every line | `frame.rs` `deactivated_instances_are_skipped_everywhere`, `destroyed_instances_deactivate_and_outlast_the_cleanup`; `collision.rs` `queries_return_the_first_match_in_list_order`; [`gm8_probe.rs`](../crates/gm/tests/gm8_probe.rs) `deactivation_order` |
| **Persistent instances: GM8 carries only the active, undestroyed ones, in its array's order.** Leaving a room (Spelunky.exe 0x53e270), GM8 runs the cleanup (0x4aefb0), then the room end events, then scans its array of active instances (0x4ad5e8) for the persistent ones (+0xca) that aren't destroyed (+0x108) and copies each into the next room in that order (0x4ab45c, 0x4ab670); `game_end` runs the same routine. OpenGMK (`game.rs` `load_room`, `instancelist.rs` `remove_as_vec`) ran no cleanup and carried every persistent instance, in slot order: deactivated ones stayed deactivated, destroyed ones arrived destroyed (and counted in `instance_count` until the next cleanup). So, on GM8 and not on OpenGMK (probe `persistent-carry`): a deactivated persistent instance, or one destroyed in the step that changes the room or in a room end event, doesn't travel; the room end events don't count the instances destroyed before them; and the rest arrive by depth, those created or reactivated since the last draw last. **Fixed in the oracle and the port (851-3315):** `InstanceList::take_persistent` and `World::load_room`'s cleanup, patch 0033. | **In play, at most level exits:** `oMusic` (persistent, at (0, 0), no sprite) isn't on `oLevel`'s reactivation list, so whenever the view is more than 96 px from the room's corner `oLevel` deactivates it, and GM8 drops it at the next room change. Nothing makes another (it's placed only in `rScreenInit`), and the music lives in globals, so a player hears no difference; only its game end event (`SS_Unload`) no longer runs when the game quits. The other persistent controllers, which `oLevel` reactivates every step, arrive in a new order. 69 of the 126 fingerprints changed; no replay's room timeline moved. | [GM8](../tools/gm8-probe/README.md) on a real Intel CPU and under Rosetta (`persistent-carry`, line for line the same); the runner's code; the patched oracle matches every line | `rooms.rs` `only_active_persistent_instances_leave_in_draw_order`; [`gm8_probe.rs`](../crates/gm/tests/gm8_probe.rs) `persistent_carry` |
| **Points and rectangles against a box.** OpenGMK takes a point's box as `left <= x <= right` and rounds a `collision_rectangle`'s corners to integers (cut to 32 bits) before comparing them; GM8 compares both unrounded as `left <= v < right + 1`, picks the rectangle's smaller and larger corner from the reals, and rounds the corners only for a precise test's pixels (§5). **Fixed in the oracle and the port (851-3306):** patch 0027 and `gm::collision` do what GM8 does. | 23 of the 126 replays' fingerprints changed: a point at a fractional position in a box's last column or row now meets it, and the game's 546 `collision_point` calls make that common (the ground, wall and ceiling checks). Before, the port and the oracle agreed with each other and both differed from 1.1. | [GM8](../tools/gm8-probe/README.md) on a real Intel CPU and under Rosetta (`collision-edges`, `collision-edges-trap`), and the runner's code | [`gm8_probe.rs`](../crates/gm/tests/gm8_probe.rs), `collision.rs` |
| **The motion setters.** OpenGMK recomputes `hspeed` and `vspeed` on every `speed` and `direction` write, even of the value already there (GM8 skips it), wraps a direction with `rem_euclid` (GM8 with Delphi's 32-bit `Floor`, which wraps from 360 * 2^31 and traps from 2^63 * 360), takes `atan2` for `hspeed` -0 (GM8 gives 0, 90 or 270 for `hspeed` 0), keeps a direction snapped to 360 (GM8 wraps it to 0), and skips the update after gravity or `motion_add` when neither component changed (GM8 always does it): §5. **Fixed in the oracle and the port (851-3280):** patch 0020 and `gm::Instance` do what GM8 does. Writing `vspeed` doesn't recompute `hspeed` on GM8, as 851-3262 suspected from `angle-motion-add`: the -2^-53 there is GM8's reading of the literal `0.75`, which the port and the oracle already share (§3). | None between port and oracle: both answer `motion-setters`, `direction-2p63` and `divassign-skip` as GM8 on Intel does, line for line. No fingerprint changed: the game writes only `speed` and `direction` (§5 of `docs/source-inventory.md`), with directions in (-90, 270), so its components always come from them and a skipped write would have recomputed the same values. | [GM8](../tools/gm8-probe/README.md) on a real Intel CPU and under Rosetta (`motion-setters`, `direction-2p63`, `divassign-skip`) | [`gm8_probe.rs`](../crates/gm/tests/gm8_probe.rs), `instance.rs` |
| **How long a string `real * string` can build.** GM8's runner is a 32-bit process, and a long enough string runs it out of memory (§2). Where depends on its free address space, not on a rule: under Wine, on a real Intel CPU and under Rosetta, it builds 304 MiB of `"a"` and stops at 312 MiB, the same on every run; a real Windows runner, with another layout, may stop elsewhere. **Decision (851-3282):** the port and the oracle stop past 304 MiB, the longest length measured to work (`gm8::REPEAT_MAX_LEN`, in `gm8.rs`, so patch 0014 has the same limit). Before, they built up to `i32::MAX` bytes: 2 GiB from `2147483647 * "a"`, which a wasm32 build can't survive failing to allocate. | None in play: the game repeats a string at most a few dozen times. Past 304 MiB and below wherever a real runner gives out, the port and the oracle stop where it might go on. And the limit is on one result, where GM8's is the whole address space: two 300 MiB strings, or `s + s` of one, would run GM8 out of memory but not the port or the oracle. **Decision (851-3284): accepted, not modelled.** A budget would have to count every live string's bytes (variables, arrays, temporaries, and Delphi's reference-counted strings, shared by assignment, once each) and follow how GM8's `+` reallocates as a string grows, all against an edge that is one runner's free address space rather than a rule, so it couldn't be exact. The game never builds a string anywhere near this size, so `+` keeps no limit in the port and the oracle. | [GM8](../tools/gm8-probe/README.md) on a real Intel CPU and under Rosetta (`mulstr-*` probes) | [`value::tests::real_times_string_stops_where_gm8_runs_out_of_memory`](../crates/gm/src/value.rs), [`gm8_probe.rs`](../crates/gm/tests/gm8_probe.rs) |
| **`max`/`min` with a NaN**: GM8's `max(nan, 1)` is NaN and `min(nan, 1)` is 1 (Delphi's own comparisons, where a NaN is "less" than anything); OpenGMK gives the other. `arccos(2)`, `log2(0)`, `log10(0)` and `logn(2, 0)` are errors on OpenGMK and on GM8 on a real Intel CPU ("Error in function log10()."; under Rosetta they're NaN and -inf). | None: the port has none of these functions, as the game never calls them. | [GM8](../tools/gm8-probe/README.md) (`ops`, `arccos2`, `log10-0`, `log2-0`, `logn-0` probes) | none |

GM8's is the x87's; unpatched, OpenGMK's is glibc's, which is 1 ulp off the correctly rounded result on 12-16% of the fractional gaps `oVampire`'s `distance_to_point` gets (on whole gaps, all `distance_to_object` sees, every implementation is correctly rounded). Apple's libm, the `libm` crate and upstream pxfm are each wrong on different inputs. **Decision (851-3174):** both use our correctly rounded `f_hypot` in the vendored pxfm, the port through `gm::math::hypot` and the oracle through patch 0008. | As above: none between port and oracle; `oVampire`'s `dist` may differ from real GM8 in the last bit. | Oracle (`tools/math-probe`: our `hypot` matches mpmath on all ~7.5M inputs, with the same hashes natively, on wasm32 and in the oracle image) | [`math::tests::hypot_is_correctly_rounded`](../crates/gm/src/math.rs) |

The port's `audio` crate has `Mode::OracleCompat` to answer like the stub for oracle diffs (§8). | OpenGMK (`game/external.rs` dummy externals, enabled by patch `0003`) | `crates/audio/src/ss.rs` `oracle_compat_answers` |
| **The highscore table is ours** (the real one is on disk); its fresh contents (ten `<nobody>` entries scoring 0) match GM8's, and so does `highscore_add`. The game only compares values with its offsets and never reads names. Patch 0009 dumps it at the end of a run, so `fidelity` compares the port's with it. | Unlikely to matter: the game reads places 1-10. | Confirmed against [GM8](../tools/gm8-probe/README.md) (`rng` probe) | none |
| **GM8's number rules where OpenGMK had its own.** Real GM8 prints reals with Delphi's `FloatToStrF` (`string` and `string_format`), makes `/ 0`, `div 0` and `mod 0` errors (and `/= 0` a no-op), makes `exp` and `power` errors where the result isn't finite (as OpenGMK already did for `sqrt`, `arcsin` and `ln`), makes `!=`, `>` and `>=` true for a NaN difference, wraps `floor`, `ceil`, `div` (and so `mod`) to 32 bits, makes `round`, `floor`, `ceil`, `div`, `mod` and the bitwise operators errors past the `Int64` range (and `string()` there `"ERROR"`), and does bitwise operators on signed 64-bit integers, with x86 shift counts. OpenGMK did none of these. **Fixed in the oracle (851-3246):** patch 0014 implements each rule as the port does (§3 and `gm::math`), with the arithmetic in a copy of the port's `crates/gm/src/gm8.rs` that `tools/oracle/sync-gm8-arith` writes into the patch (`scripts/check` fails if it's stale). Where GM8 under Rosetta and on a real Intel CPU disagree (the x87's exceptions, which Rosetta doesn't trap), both follow Intel. | None between port and oracle: the port gives the oracle's answer on every line the oracle printed, and stops wherever the oracle stopped. Both match GM8 on Intel on every line it printed but the transcendentals' (above), and GM8 under Rosetta on every line but those where Intel traps. The traces didn't change (none hits these cases). | [GM8](../tools/gm8-probe/README.md) vs the patched oracle (`python3 -I tools/gm8-probe/compare.py`) | [`gm8_probe.rs`](../crates/gm/tests/gm8_probe.rs) |
| **The x87's exceptions, and values past doubles' range.** On a real Intel CPU GM8 traps the x87's invalid-operation, zero-divide and overflow exceptions (851-3247), and 851-3259 probed what that reaches beyond the math functions: arithmetic and comparisons that overflow, integers past the `Int64` range, both inside an `op=`, `real()` and literals all stop the game (§3), where Rosetta, which doesn't trap, gives ±inf and -2^63. **Fixed in the oracle and the port (851-3259):** patch 0014 and `gm::math` follow Intel. So GML on Intel never makes an infinity or a NaN, and the rules for them (`!=`, `>` and `>=` true for a NaN difference, `sign(NaN) == 1`, `string()`'s and `string_format`'s `"INF"` and `"NAN"`, what a `repeat` or `chr` makes of one) are Rosetta's, kept because they're harmless. **The motion snap (851-3283):** the runner snaps each motion component to a whole number within 0.0001 through Delphi's `Round` into an `Int64` (§5), which traps from 2^63 up and below -2^63 (-2^63 itself fits). So on Intel GM8 stops on `speed = 2^63` or `-2^63 - 2048`, a `direction` write that turns a speed of 2^63 onto an axis, any nonzero speed at a direction past 2^63 radians (`fsin` and `fcos` return such an angle unchanged, so the components pass 2^63; which is why `direction-2p63` sets the speed to 0 first), `motion_set` (the two setters), `lengthdir_x(2^63, 0)` and `lengthdir_y(2^63, 270)`, and on `motion_add`'s speed as on an `hspeed` write's (851-3281). Probes `speedwrite-2p63`, `speedwrite-neg2p63`, `direction-turn-2p63`, `direction-radians-2p63`, `hspeed-neg2p63`, `motion-set-2p63`, `motion-add-2p63`, `lengthdir-2p63`, `lengthdir-y-2p63`, `lengthdir-neg2p63`. **Fixed in the oracle and the port (851-3283):** the setters and `lengthdir` return `gm::math::snap_error` where it traps, storing Rosetta's unsnapped components, and patch 0021 does the same in OpenGMK (and `motion_add`'s speed check, as the port has no `motion_add`). **Where GM8 doesn't stop (851-3290):** every trap above, like every `errors/` probe before it, was measured in the first room's create events, at game start. Later a trap shows no dialog: GM8 on Intel drops the rest of the frame and goes on, and so does running out of memory, where GML's own errors still show their dialog (`late-*` probes, `step-trap`, `step-trap-order`, `gravity-overflow`, `move-overflow`; §5). **Fixed in the oracle and the port (851-3290):** `World::step` and patch 0022 catch these exceptions in the main loop as GM8 does, and the step's friction, gravity and move trap where GM8's do, storing what GM8 stores before each trap (§5); OpenGMK stopped the game, and ran the step's friction and gravity through unchecked. **The rest of the runner's own arithmetic (851-3301):** read from the runner's code and measured on Intel and under Rosetta (probes `anim-trap`, `draw-floor-trap`, `sprite-floor`, `bbox-trap`, `bbox-scale`, `bbox-place`, `bbox-move`, `bbox-create`, `bg-trap`, `view-trap`; §5): the animation's sum and its `Floor` of `image_index`, `sprite_index`'s `Floor`, the default draw's `Floor`, the bounding box's `Round`s and rotated offsets in every setter that recomputes it, the backgrounds' scroll and the views' `Round` of their targets all trap on Intel, storing first what GM8 stores; the alarms' countdown is integer and doesn't. **Fixed in the oracle and the port (851-3301):** `gm` (`World::animate`, `World::check_bbox`, `View::follow`, ...) and patch 0023 do the same, OpenGMK having stored infinities and saturated integers, ignored the default draw's errors, and computed the box only when read; both answer the ten probes as GM8 on Intel does, line for line. A `Trunc` that traps (Delphi's `Floor`) leaves the x87 rounding to nearest afterwards, as before (`anim-trap`'s `1 / 10`), so no rounding mode carries over. **The precise collision tests (851-3305):** each takes Delphi's `Floor` of `image_index` for its frame, one rule for every test (OpenGMK had two, §5), and rounds positions, points and line ends into an `Int64`, all of which trap on Intel once the boxes meet (probes `collision-frame`, `collision-frame-trap`); **fixed in the oracle and the port:** `gm::collision` and patch 0024 do the same, line for line. A rectangle's corners are rounded (and trap) only in a precise test whose box meets the rectangle (851-3306, `collision-edges-trap`; OpenGMK, and so the oracle before patch 0027, converted them for every call, so a corner at 2^64 stopped even a non-precise test). **Drawing's own conversions (851-3304):** read from the runner's code and measured on Intel and under Rosetta (probes `draw-builtin-trap`, `draw-default-trap`, `draw-stage-trap`; §5): a draw's corners stored as singles (from 2^128 - 2^103), the scaled draw's offsets as doubles, `draw_sprite`'s `Floor` of `image_index`, `draw_text`'s `Round` of a real, `draw_rectangle`'s `Floor`s, a background layer's `Round`s and a view's projection all trap on Intel; Mesa's renderer under Wine adds no trap of its own (corners at the largest single draw). **Fixed in the oracle and the port (851-3304):** the rules are `gm8.rs`'s, which the port checks whether or not it records the frame (it had computed `draw_sprite`'s `Floor` only then) and patch 0028 runs through patch 0014's copy; OpenGMK had drawn whatever it was given, and `draw_text` of 2^63 as `"ERROR"`. Both answer the three probes as GM8 on Intel does, line for line. Doubles stand in for the x87's register exactly here: where a register would go past the largest double, a corner of the same draw ends past the largest single, so the draw traps either way. The probe also showed two drawing differences, unrelated to traps: GM8 draws nothing for a character the font lacks, and nothing for `draw_surface_stretched` with a width or height of 0 or less (no trap at -2T); **fixed in the oracle and the renderer (851-3308, §6)**. Not modelled: GM8's iteration also skips its message pump, so input that arrives during a dropped frame reaches the game a frame late; a replay holds what each frame saw. A dropped frame is shown only if GML presented it (`screen_refresh`); GM8 presents at the end of its draw stage, so one dropped by an animation-end event was shown there (from the runner's structure; not measured). How far running out of memory gets depends on the address space left (`late-mulstr`: a 304 MiB string, which fits at game start, no longer did after a 312 MiB one failed). Still different too: a literal past the largest double (`1e309`) stops GM8 on Intel while it loads, where the port and the oracle read it as inf (no game literal comes near). And an instance created where its bounding box traps (`instance_create` at x = 2^63, §5) leaves GM8 on Intel with a hole in its instance list, empty or stale, which faults every later frame before any event (`bbox-create`: no line after the trap, and a black screen until the timeout); there's nothing to model past that, so the port (`Error::CreationTrap`) and the oracle stop the game there. Not modelled either: a `sprite_index` write whose `Floor` traps (`image_index` from 2^63) has stored the sprite but not recomputed the box, so GM8 keeps the old sprite's box until its next setter, where the port and the oracle, which compute the box from the instance's inputs, take the new sprite's (from the runner's code; not measured). `string_format(1, 2^32 + 4, 1)` hangs GM8 under Rosetta (its width isn't cut to 32 bits). | None known: the game makes no infinities, NaNs or values past 2^63 in the traces so far, and no fingerprint changed (851-3283, 851-3290). | [GM8](../tools/gm8-probe/README.md) on a real Intel CPU and under Rosetta (the `errors/` probes, `int-args`, `underflow`, `string-format-big`, `friction-snap`) | [`gm8_probe.rs`](../crates/gm/tests/gm8_probe.rs), `instance.rs` |
| **No joystick**: OpenGMK reports none, so `oGamepad` sees nothing even though the shipped `settings.cfg` enables the gamepad. The port gives the same stub answers (`joystick_exists` false, `joystick_direction` 101, `joystick_pov` -1, axes 0) whenever the replay has no `joystick` directive. | Gamepad input can't be checked against the oracle, so what a connected joystick reports is assumed: `joystick_direction` counts an axis past ±0.5, and the app reads a pad as winmm reads an XInput pad (A, B, X, Y, LB, RB, back, start as buttons 1-8; z is left trigger minus right; the d-pad is the POV hat). | OpenGMK (`gml/kernel.rs` `joystick_*`) | [`without_a_joystick_the_builtins_answer_like_the_oracle`](../crates/game/src/builtins.rs) |
| **Text in GM's built-in font draws nothing**: the port doesn't ship that font, so `draw_text` before any `draw_set_font` (or after one with an invalid id) records no command. Only the unused `core` object draws text that way. | Unreachable in play; the frame would differ from an oracle screenshot. | GML (`core` is the only unit that draws text without `draw_set_font`) | `drawing.rs` `text_in_the_built_in_font_records_nothing` |
| **`game_end`**: OpenGMK's replay loop handles it by restarting the game (`game.rs`, the replay loop's `SceneChange::End` arm) and playing on. **Fixed in the oracle (851-3190):** with `OPENGMK_EXIT_AT_END`, patch 0010 runs the room end and game end events and stops after dumping that frame, as GM8 and the port do. | None: the traces, files (`stats.txt`) and highscore table match through the `game_end` frame (`fidelity/replays/ending-seed-1.txt`). | OpenGMK; oracle (`ending-seed-1.txt`) | `rooms.rs` `game_end_runs_room_end_then_game_end_and_stops` |
| **`file_find_first`**: OpenGMK globs the mask without turning its `\` into `/`, so on Linux the game's `levels\*.lvl` (`oLoadLevel`) would look for files literally named `levels\….lvl` and find none, and it lists matches in glob's byte order. GM8 lists `levels/` in NTFS's order (upper-cased, so `ab` < `alpha` < `a_b`). The port does what GM8 does: matching names (`*` and `?`, ignoring case) in the mask's directory, in upper-cased order. **Fixed in the oracle (851-3245):** patch 0011 converts `\` to `/` off Windows and sorts the names upper-cased. | None: the load-level menu lists, pages through and loads the same levels on both (`fidelity/replays/mouse-load-level.txt`, twelve levels on two pages). | OpenGMK (`gml/kernel.rs` `file_find_first`: `glob_with(path, ...)` on the unconverted path); oracle (`mouse-load-level.txt`) | `files.rs` `find_lists_matching_names_in_windows_order` |
| **`op=` type errors on built-ins and arguments**: OpenGMK compiles `op=` on a built-in variable or an argument as strict (`gml/compiler.rs` `make_modify_instruction`, `type_unsafe: false`), so `x += "a"` or `argument0 -= 1` on a string stops the game. GM8 keeps the old value without an error, as it does on fields (§2). **Fixed in the oracle (851-3256):** patch 0012 makes them lenient too, and the port follows. Whether GM8 then writes the kept value back through the variable's setter can't be seen from GML (on GM8 even `speed = speed` leaves `hspeed` bit for bit); the port and the oracle do, as for fields. A string assigned to a real built-in (`x = "abc"`) reads back 0, and a real assigned to a string one (`room_caption = 7`) `""`, on GM8 and OpenGMK alike. | None in the traces: no replay type-errors in an `op=`. Before the fix the oracle and the port stopped where GM8 goes on. | GM8 (`opassign` probe in `tools/gm8-probe/results/`; the committed OpenGMK results are the patched oracle's, identical to GM8's). Upstream OpenGMK stops at the probe's first line, `Runtime error: invalid operands real and string to + operator (5 + "a")`, as `make_modify_instruction` in `gml/compiler.rs` says | [`compound_assignment_is_lenient_on_every_name`](../crates/transpile/tests/emit.rs) |
| **File names are case-sensitive on the oracle** (Linux), not on Windows. The port follows the oracle. | None in play: the game always names a file in the same case (`levels/*.lvl` names are lower-cased first). | OpenGMK (`std::fs` on Linux) | none |
| **`file_text_close` of a handle in 1-32 that isn't open** is an error on OpenGMK (its flush check fails first), though its own comment says GM8 ignores it; the port ignores it. | Unreachable: the game only closes files it opened. | OpenGMK (`gml/kernel.rs` `file_text_close`) | `files.rs` `bad_handles_are_errors` |
| Unimplemented functions panic: `show_message`, `show_menu`, `show_info` and `get_integer`, all only in the demo object `core`; `show_error` stops the game even with `abort` false. | Unreachable in play. The port answers as if each dialog were dismissed at once (`show_menu` returns its default rounded to an item index, which is assumed; `get_integer` returns its default) and, like the oracle, stops on `show_error` (only in the overridden `SS_Init`). | OpenGMK | `drawing.rs` `dialogs_answer_as_if_dismissed_at_once` |
| **`execute_program`** (only in the settings program: OK with "start game on exit" checked runs `execute_program("Spelunky.exe", 0, false)`, then `game_end()`). GM8 starts the game as a separate process. OpenGMK spawns the command line too, which fails on the oracle (no `Spelunky.exe` it can execute on Linux) with a GML error. **Port deviation:** the port starts no executable; the call is recorded in the world (`game::HostState::executed`, so savestates carry it) and returns 0, and when the settings program ends the frontend starts the game in the same window, on the session's header (seed, clock, joystick) and the game directory the settings program left, so it reads the new settings (`app::settings`). The settings program's sound stops then, as its process would. Waiting (`wait` true) is an error. | Doesn't touch either program's simulation: the settings program ends in the same step. No replay can compare it, since the oracle errors there. | port decision | `crates/config/tests/settings.rs`, `app` `settings.rs` tests |

## 8. SuperSound DLL

All sound goes through `supersound.dll`. Its behaviour comes from its disassembly and the `SS_*` GML wrappers;
anything inferred rather than observed is marked "verify" in the `audio` crate docs. Port: "model" is
`model.rs` (`AudioModel`), "wrappers" is `ss.rs` (`SuperSound::call`); tests are in those files.

| Quirk | Where | Status | Port | Test |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `SS_Unload` calls the DLL's `SS_Init` (§1), which starts a new sound table: every handle stops working. The DLL's paused list survives, so a sound paused before still reads as paused. | `scripts/SS_Init.gml`; DLL `SS_Init` | GML + DLL | model `init` | `init_again_invalidates_every_handle`, `the_paused_list_survives_init` |
| `SS_LoadSound` returns `"No"` for a missing file, not `"0"` as its wrapper says. | DLL `SS_LoadSound` | DLL | model `load_sound` | `faithful_answers` |
| `SS_SetSoundPosition` returns 2 on success and 1 for an unknown handle: the wrapper adds 1 to the DLL's `"1"`/`"0"`, though its comment promises 1 and 0. | `scripts/SS_SetSoundPosition.gml` | GML | wrappers | `faithful_answers` |
| `SS_GetSoundVol` of an unknown handle is 9999: the DLL's `"-1"` plus the wrapper's 10000. | `scripts/SS_GetSoundVol.gml`; DLL `SS_GetSoundVol` | GML + DLL | wrappers | `faithful_answers` |
| `SS_SetSoundVol`, `SS_SetSoundPan`, `SS_SetSoundFreq` and `SS_SetSoundPosition` report success for any known handle, even when DirectSound rejects the value and nothing changes (volume outside -10000..0, pan outside ±10000, frequency outside 100..200000 except 0, a position past the buffer). | DLL setters ignore the `HRESULT` | DLL | model `set_sound_*` | `setters_ignore_values_directsound_rejects` |
| Numeric arguments reach the DLL as GML `string()`s and are read with PureBasic's `Val()`, which keeps the integer part: the music volume 8666.67 becomes `"-1333.33"` and then -1333. | DLL (every numeric argument) | DLL | `ss.rs` `pb_val` | `val_parses_like_purebasic` |
| A static (WAV) sound that played to its end reads position 0. | DirectSound | Assumed (verify) | model `tick` | `a_static_sound_stops_at_its_end_and_rewinds` |
| `SS_ResumeSound` doesn't start a stopped sound, despite its wrapper's comment: the DLL only resumes sounds in its paused list. | DLL `SS_ResumeSound` | DLL | model `resume_sound` | `pause_and_resume` |
| A playing stream (the music) always reports looping, because its DirectSound ring buffer loops even when the track plays once. Its position and length are the 64 KiB ring's. So a paused stream resumes looping, from the start of the file with its ring cursor moved. | DLL streaming | DLL | model | `streams_report_their_ring_buffer`, `a_paused_stream_resumes_looping_from_its_ring_cursor` |
| A stream that plays once ends at the next half-buffer boundary after the end of the file, padded with silence. | DLL refill thread | DLL | model `tick` | `a_stream_stops_at_the_half_buffer_after_its_end` |
| Return types are inconsistent: for a real (invalid) handle `SS_GetSoundVol` returns the string `"-1"` and `SS_GetSoundPan` the string `"0"`, the other queries reals. | `scripts/SS_*.gml` | GML | wrappers | `faithful_answers` |
| Under the oracle, OpenGMK's stub makes `SS_LoadSound` return 0, so every wrapper takes its `is_real` branch: `SS_IsSoundPlaying`, `SS_IsSoundLooping`, `SS_IsSoundPaused`, `SS_GetSoundFreq`, `SS_GetSoundPosition`, `SS_PauseSound` and `SS_ResumeSound` answer -1. Reproduced by `Mode::OracleCompat`, for oracle diffs only (see §7). | OpenGMK `external.rs` `should_dummy`; `scripts/SS_*.gml` | OpenGMK + GML | wrappers | `oracle_compat_answers` |
